# Run 08 — every research lane and its verification

_AI-assisted research input for the team, disclosed as such under the competition rules. Not submission text. Every figure carries a source so a teammate can re-check it._


---

## Power margin: mix vs within-category (services vs equipment) decomposition

**Headline:** The Power margin climb from FY23 to H1'26 is not a mix effect. Equipment's share of revenue rose (32.1% to 36.9%), which pulled the margin down by 0.5 to 2.8 pts, so the whole gain came from within categories. Whether pre-2025 equipment earns about GLJ's ~3 pts depends on a services margin GEV does not disclose (H1'26 equipment is 3% if services are 26.2%, and 13.6% if services are 20%). GEV's own backlog contribution-margin disclosures (Power +3/+3/+11 pts, average basis) are stronger evidence than the decomposition, and GLJ's '3 vs 10-11' matches those year-over-year increments rather than vintage levels.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** 45 facts checked: 43 verified and 2 corrected. Every segment figure, driver quote, organic reconciliation, income-statement ratio, unit table and Siemens Energy figure matched its source. Grids A, B, C and E recompute exactly.

The one material correction is PMX-29 and Grid D. The roll-forward used reported YE22/YE23 Power equipment RPO, which still includes the backlog sold to EDF. Slide 25 of the Q4'24 deck gives the EDF equipment RPO as $3,771M at YE22 and $3,708M at YE23, and slide 5's margin increments are measured on this adjusted basis. On the adjusted basis the 2024 vintage is about b+7.5 contribution, not b+11.2 (about b+5.5 under FIFO). The 2023 vintage is about b+5.2 to 5.9, and the 2025 vintage is unchanged at about b+20.4.

PMX-35's range depends on an unstated Power services GM level. Across 25-40% it is -10.6 to -22.6 pts, and the direction holds.

Smaller points:
- The Grid A footnote 'Steam moved out' is wrong.
- PMX-31's services-pricing mechanism is from CFO Parks, not CEO Strazik.
- PMX-30's >20% order pricing is partly aeroderivative and combined-cycle mix.
- The GEV vs Siemens Energy margin comparison mixes definitions (segment EBITDA vs profit before special items plus D&A including impairment).
- The Power +3/+3/+11 slide values were independently re-derived from coordinates and match the Q4'24 deck.

### Tests

| test | method | result | if long | if short |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Is the Power margin climb a MIX effect (services share rising)? | Laspeyres split of margin change into mix=(w1-w0)(e0-s0) and within-category, with services margin s0 on a 20-35% grid; periods FY23 to FY25, FY24 to FY25 and H1'25r to H1'26 | No. The services share FELL (67.9% to 66.2% to 63.1%). Because equipment carries the lower margin, the mix effect is negative in every row: -0.54 to -1.34 pts (FY23 to FY25) and -0.80 to -2.84 pts (H1/H1). The entire climb, and more, is within-category. The rival case must therefore be 'services margin rose', not 'mix'. | The rival as literally framed (mix) fails on arithmetic. The team can say the climb happened while mix went against it. | The short case has to argue services-margin expansion, not mix, and needs a services-margin number to do so. |
| At what services margin does implied H1'26 Power equipment EBITDA margin fall to GLJ's ~3 pts? | e=(EBITDA-sxS)/E on H1'26 recast figures; grid s=15-35% | e=3% when s=26.2%. e=5.0% at s=25%, 13.6% at s=20%, 22.1% at s=15%, and negative at s=30% (-3.6%). e>=10% needs s<=22.1%. For FY25: e=3% at s=20.7%; e=4.3% at s=20%. | Equipment is clearly above GLJ only if Power services EBITDA margin is at or below about 22%. GEV does not disclose this, so the team must choose and defend the assumption. | GLJ's ~3 pts is arithmetically consistent with Power services near 26% EBITDA in H1'26. That level is not contradicted by any GEV disclosure. |

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- Test 6 (vintages) and Grid D are WRONG as built. They use reported YE22/YE23 Power equipment RPO (13,579/13,636), which includes EDF-sold backlog. GEV's own Q4'24 deck slide 25 gives EDF equipment RPO of $3,771M (YE22) and $3,708M (YE23), and slide 5's increments are measured on the adjusted basis. Corrected b=0 vintages: 2023 about 5.2-5.9, 2024 about 7.5-7.6, 2025 about 20.4. For Grid D rows b=-3/0/+3, the 2024 vintage becomes about 4.5 / 7.5 / 10.5. The if_long line 'the 2024 vintage is roughly 11 pts of contribution' must become 'about 7.5' (about 5.5 under a FIFO variant). That is still above GLJ's ~3, but closer. The 'unresolved' item saying the EDF-adjusted segment figure 'was not found' is now resolved.
- The roll-forward carries the unshipped old book at the prior-year AVERAGE margin. If shipments come from the oldest, lowest-margin backlog first, the implied new vintage is lower: 2024 about b+5.5, 2025 about b+19.3. The roll-forward also ignores repricing and cost re-estimates of existing backlog, which also move the 'average estimated contribution margin'. These should be listed as method limits.
- Test 1 (mix) is sound. The 'negative in every scenario' result is conditional on s0 exceeding the segment margin (true on the 20-35% grid; company services GM of about 30% vs equipment about 10% supports it). State the condition.
- Test 4 (company services GM cross-check): the stated range -10.6 to -20.9 depends on an unstated Power services GM level. Across x = 25-40% it is -10.6 to -22.6. The test also compares an EBITDA-margin change with a gross-margin change; D&A sits in cost of services, and Power services revenue includes intersegment sales. The 'circumstantial' label is appropriate.
- Test 5 (reported vs organic): chaining the +1.8 and +1.0 organic changes across different base restatements is approximate, as the lane says. The '~40% non-organic' figure (2.0/4.8 = 42%) partly reflects removing a depressed FY23 base (FX -$118M), not only gains in FY25. The if_short wording 'not pricing' overreaches.
- Test 7 (peer): the GEV 14.7% vs SE 14.5% 'almost identical' comparison mixes definitions. GEV reports segment EBITDA; SE's proxy is profit before special items plus amortization, depreciation AND impairment. SE's own reported margin is 13.0%. Also, SE's '~40%/~60%' labels refer to FY25 orders mix, not revenue.
- PMX-30: the >20% order pricing is partly mix (more aeroderivatives and combined-cycle scope, per the same paragraph). Do not treat it as like-for-like price.
- The Grid A footnote 'Steam moved out' is incorrect. Steam Power was realigned within Power; the recast adds an Electrification Software component to Gas Power services only (H1'25 +53 revenue, +17 EBITDA; equipment unchanged).
- PMX-31 attribution: the services-pricing mechanism is CFO Parks. The 'Both are experiencing real margin accretion' line is CEO Strazik. Cite each correctly.
- The 4Q'24 deck said 'Delivering majority of our 2023/2024 orders in 2026+'. The 4Q'25 deck says '2024/2025 orders in 2027+'. This timing slip may matter for the vintage narrative.

### Unresolved

- GEV does not disclose Power services or equipment EBITDA (or gross) margins, or a segment-level cost split. Every row in Grid A depends on an assumed services margin that is the team's choice.
- Slide 5 (4Q'25 deck) values were assigned to segment and year from PDF text coordinates, then cross-checked against three independent totals. The team should confirm visually on the slide before citing Power +3/+3/+11.
- The gap between backlog 'contribution margin' and realized segment EBITDA margin (fixed manufacturing overhead, SG&A, R&D) is not disclosed. The vintage levels in Grid D cannot be compared directly with the EBITDA-based Grid A.
- YE2022 base is only 'directionally approximately breakeven' (CEO agreeing with an analyst's framing). No numeric base is disclosed. The YE2023 Power figure should use EDF-adjusted equipment backlog, which was not found by segment; the reported $13,636M was used.
- GLJ's own derivation of '~3 points (2024 book) vs 10-11 (2025 vintage)' was not accessed. The match to slide 5's +3/+11 increments is a coincidence of numbers, not a verified error.
- No recast FY2025 annual Power figures (2026 perimeter) were found in the corpus; only H1'25 and quarterly recasts. The FY23-FY25 vs 2026 series therefore mixes perimeters (effect about 0.1 pt on H1'25).
- Power 'equipment' includes Nuclear, Hydro and (pre-2026) Steam equipment, not just gas turbines. Gas Power was 81% of FY25 Power revenue (16,006/19,767). The gas-only equipment margin is not isolable.
- Siemens Energy 'profit before special items' is EBITA-type. Adding segment 'amortization, depreciation and impairment' approximates EBITDA but may not match GEV's segment EBITDA definition exactly. SE fiscal year ends 30 September.
- Baker Hughes FY25 full-year Gas Tech Equipment/Services revenue ($6,619M/$3,028M) appeared only in a search summary and was not verified; only Q4'25 lines were confirmed.
- The recast Q3'25 Power comparative (needed for the 2026-10-28 print) is not yet published.

### Corrections to the verified state

- Power services share of segment revenue is 68.5% (FY24) to 66.2% (FY25), not 68.4% to 66.1%: 12,419/18,127=68.51%; 13,081/19,767=66.18% (FY25 10-K MD&A Power table).
- The Power margin series mixes perimeters. FY23 9.88%, FY24 12.51% and FY25 14.68% are on the pre-2026 basis. H1'26 17.63% and Q2'26 18.82% are on the 2026 realigned basis (Steam Power folded into Nuclear/Hydro/Gas, plus an Electrification Software component moved into Gas Power; Q1'26 10-Q Note 1). The like-for-like comparison is H1'25 recast 14.11% vs H1'26 17.63%; the original H1'25 was 14.01%.
- '2025 one-year conversion Power 53.7%' is FY25 Power equipment revenue divided by YE2024 Power equipment RPO (6,686/12,461=53.66%). It is not an RPO-note disclosure, and it assumes all 2025 equipment revenue came from the YE2024 backlog.
- Reported FY23 to FY25 Power margin expansion of 4.8 pts includes about 2.0 pts of non-organic effects. GEV organic tables show +1.8 pts (FY24) and +1.0 pt (FY25). FX alone added $107M to FY25 Power EBITDA (FY25 10-K p.34).
- GLJ's '~3 points (2024 book) vs 10-11 points (2025 vintage)' matches the Power year-over-year INCREMENTS on GEV's 4Q'25 slide 5 (+3 pts FY24, +11 pts FY25). The slide footnote defines these as changes in AVERAGE estimated contribution margin in backlog, not vintage margin levels. On the same disclosures the 2024 vintage works out to about 11 pts of contribution margin (Grid D). This is unverified against GLJ's report.

---

## GLJ — pin the bear's exact claims and run GLJ's own vintage inputs through the RPO conversion shares

**Headline:** GLJ's "3 pts vs 10-11 pts" almost certainly comes from GEV's own slide of Power backlog contribution-margin expansion (FY24 +3, FY25 +11). Those numbers are year-over-year changes in the average of the whole backlog, not margins on each year's orders. GLJ maps 2024 orders to 2027 shipments and 2025 orders to 2028. Re-weighting GLJ's own numbers by the RPO schedule lifts the 2027 blend from 3 to 9.3-13.6 pts. Even so, that closes only about $0.6-1.3B of GLJ's $2.03B EBITDA gap. No public rebuttal using the RPO schedule was found.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** I checked 36 facts: 29 verified, 3 corrected (GLJ-03, 16, 26) and 4 definition-mismatch (GLJ-25, 30, and partial issues in 16/26), with none left unverifiable. All filing figures hold and tie out to the 10-K/10-Q text: the Power RPO, Power equipment revenue, the RPO conversion sentences, and H1'26 CFOA of $10,680M with the contract-liability change of $13,695M ($14,087M appears nowhere). The deck mapping also holds: the footnote defines backlog margin as a contribution margin, and Power's accretion was +3/+3/+11. The GLJ press quotes, multiple back-solves and grid arithmetic all recompute.

The conclusion needs revising in three places.
(1) The primary Laguna transcript of 2026-09-16 quotes Strazik: the 2026 change in backlog margin 'really tells you the equipment margins for '29 and '30, largely'. That is management's own 3-4 year lag, and it supports GLJ's timing mapping.
(2) The GLJ-16 '≤$5.8B' cap is actually a floor.
(3) The claim that the RPO re-weighting closes $0.6-1.3B of GLJ's gap is inconsistent with GLJ's stated 2.2-pt Power gap (about $0.55-0.62B) and with the lane's own GLJ-28. The consistent ceiling is about $0.55-0.62B, giving a GLJ-consistent FY27 EBITDA of about $7.97-8.04B.

Primary PDFs fetched: gev_webcast_presentation_01282026.pdf p.5, gev_webcast_presentation_01222025.pdf p.6, and ge-vernova-morgan-stanley-laguna-conference-2026-transcript.pdf. Also Investing.com, and the GEV Laguna press release for the date.

### Tests

| test | method | result | if long | if short |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Which margin are GLJ's 3 and 10-11 points? | Matched the press wording against GEV's 4Q'25 slide 5 and 4Q'24 slide 6. Segment/year values were mapped from the pdfplumber word coordinates. Cross-checked against the Q4'25 call Q&A (Coe/Strazik). | The figures match Power's equipment backlog contribution-margin expansion exactly: FY24 +3, FY25 +11. They do not match the consolidated figures (+5/+6). These are year-over-year changes in the AVERAGE estimated contribution margin of the whole backlog. They are not gross, EBITDA or per-vintage margins. GLJ's own text has not been seen, so this match is an inference. | If GLJ treated a change in the backlog average as the margin level of a vintage, that is a units error the team can show with GEV's own footnote. Reading 3 and 11 as levels understates how different the vintages are, and it treats contribution margin as if it were EBITDA margin. | The size of GLJ's vintage gap (7.5 pts) sits inside what the data implies (2025 vintage about 18.5-20.4 pts versus about 6-12 for the pre-2025 remainder). The bear case holds on magnitude; the dispute is timing. |
| Is GLJ's timing mapping (2024 orders ship in 2027; 2025 orders ship in 2028) compatible with the RPO notes? | Cap tests: YE24 Power equipment RPO less FY25 Power equipment revenue, and the share of the YE24 consolidated book due beyond 2 years. | At most about $5.8B of the pre-2025 Power book remains for 2026 and 2027 combined. H1'26 Power equipment revenue alone was $3.85B. At most $10.3B of the consolidated YE24 book falls in 2027-2029. Under the consolidated schedule, 32% of the YE25 book ($20.6B) lands in 2027. A pure 3-year lag does not fit the notes. Caveat: management's own pooled statement ('majority of '24/'25 orders deliver '27+') is the likely origin of GLJ's framing, and it does not separate the two vintages. | Timing is the testable disagreement, and public filings favour a mix weighted to the 2025 vintage for 2027. The rebuttal has to be the arithmetic, not a denial, because management's own sentence points the same way as GLJ. | A bear who keeps the timing argument has to explain why pre-2025 Power backlog could fill more than about $5.8B across 2026-27, which the filings do not allow. |
| Re-weight GLJ's own vintage margins by the RPO vintage shares | Blend = s1 x 3 + s2 x 10.5 + s3 x m3, with m3 ∈ {10.5, 15.5, 20.5} (team's choice). Shares are inherited: Power pre-2025 6-16%, consolidated 8-20%, 2025 vintage 47-62%, 2026+ 29-35%. | The 2027 blended backlog margin is 9.0-13.55 pts, against GLJ's implied 3 pts. The RPO notes say about 6-20%. | The short needs a different mechanism, such as cost inflation, execution or cancellations, rather than vintage mix. |
| Translate the re-weighting into GLJ's FY27 EBITDA | Uplift = (blend - 3 pts) x 2027 Power equipment revenue ($9.0/10.5/12.0B, team's choice). That is still $0.76-1.46B below $9.45B (GLJ's Street) and $0.89-1.59B below MarketScreener's $9.574B. A clean level translation is blocked by the definition gap: backlog margin is a contribution margin, and GLJ's 18.5% is a segment EBITDA margin. Only the deltas can be translated. | The vintage re-weighting removes about 28-62% of GLJ's gap. The rest of GLJ's gap sits outside the published vintage argument (Electrification, Wind, corporate costs or revenue), so the team cannot claim to refute GLJ in full. | Most of GLJ's $2.03B gap does not rest on the vintage point. The bear case survives this test in part. |

The remaining ~$1.4-1.5B is not attributed in any public source. | When questioned, the team can say the published bear mechanism explains less than a third of the published EBITDA gap. | GLJ likely has other haircuts that are not public; the full note is needed. |

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- MATERIAL, and it cuts against the lane's conclusion. The primary Laguna transcript (2026-09-16, gevernova.com PDF) quotes Strazik: 'We'll provide our change in equipment margin in backlog in '26 ... because that really tells you the equipment margins for '29 and '30, largely.' Management itself maps a booking year's backlog-margin change to P&L margins 3-4 years later. That supports GLJ's 2024->2027 / 2025->2028 mapping. It undercuts two lane claims: 'A pure 3-year lag does not fit the notes', and the implied claim that the RPO rebuttal is uncontested. It also matches GLJ's 4-5 year cycle claim (GLJ-06). The team must address this quote before using the timing rebuttal.
- The re-weighting weights by DOLLARS, but the margin accretion sits in heavy-duty gas. RPO conversion shares cover all Power equipment (aeroderivatives, hydro, nuclear, steam, short-cycle), and GEV discloses no Power-only two-year conversion. The margin accretion is concentrated in long-lead heavy-duty gas, which Strazik maps to '29-'30. So a vintage's share of 2027 revenue is not the same as its share of 2027 margin dollars. The 9.0-13.55 pt blend can overstate how much new-vintage margin reaches 2027.
- GLJ-16 inequality points the wrong way. 12,461 - 6,686 is a floor on the pre-2025 Power remainder, not a cap, because some FY25 revenue was book-and-ship. The consolidated YE24 book predicted $18.9B of FY25 equipment revenue against $20.9B actual. The 'if_short' line ('which the filings do not allow') is therefore overstated. The only filed cap is $12.46B.
- Tables 2-3 and the headline ('closes about $0.6-1.3B of GLJ's $2.03B gap') conflict with the lane's own GLJ-27/28. GLJ's published Power margin gap to consensus is only 2.2 pts, about $0.55-0.62B. Restoring more than that would put GLJ's Power above consensus. GLJ-28 implies GLJ haircut about $7.3-8.2B of revenue, not $9-12B. The internally consistent ceiling is about $0.55-0.62B (27-30% of the gap), giving about $7.97-8.04B, not $8.69B.
- Unit inconsistency. The lane argues that GLJ's 3 and 10-11 are year-over-year changes in the book average, then blends them as vintage margin LEVELS. That is fine as an 'on GLJ's own terms' exercise, but the blend has no standalone economic meaning. The lane's own Table 4 implies a vintage spread of about 7-14 pts (2025 vintage 18.5-20.4 vs pre-2025 6-12), at least GLJ's 7.5. If the timing dispute goes GLJ's way, the magnitude is not in GLJ's favour-error.
- The 1:1 pass-through from contribution margin to EBITDA ignores fixed-cost growth from the capacity ramp (20 GW run-rate from Q3'26, then 24 GW in 2028). Treat this as an upper bound.
- The 'second independent outlet' (GLJ-03) is not independent. GuruFocus and Investing.com carry near-identical wording, and Stocktwits credits Investing.com and The Fly. Treat all of it as one secondary summary of GLJ's note.
- The GW coverage check (117%) in the verified state assumes first-in-first-out shipping of backlog. Non-FIFO slotting, with some 2024 orders for 2027-28 slots and fast-cycle aeroderivatives, can leave YE24 gas backlog shipping in 2027 even when total GW shipped exceeds the YE24 backlog. It is not a decisive test.
- The '>20%' pricing statement (GLJ-30 and the verified state) compares H1'26 orders with 4Q'25 orders, not SRAs with backlog. Management attributes part of the 2Q $/kW increase to mix and scope, so the flat-cost margin conversion (11.7-15.0 pts) is an upper bound.
- Rebuttal-search test: the scope is sound for press coverage, but it missed the primary Laguna transcript, which is public on gevernova.com. Management's public line points toward GLJ's timing, not against it.
- Minor: the corpus metadata errors are confirmed. src:2660 is dated 2026-01-23 but the call was 2026-01-28; src:3771 is labelled 2026-04-22 but is the Q3'25 call of 2025-10-22.

### Unresolved

- GLJ's own note has not been seen. That 3 / 10-11 are the Power slide's accretion figures is an inference from an exact numeric match; GLJ could have meant something else, such as absolute contribution margin by vintage.
- GLJ's FY27 revenue, segment revenue, EPS and FCF are not in any public source found, nor is anything for FY28 or the $470 method. About $1.4-1.5B of its $2.03B EBITDA gap has no public explanation.
- The 20.7% consensus Power 2027 margin and the $9.45B Street EBITDA are GLJ's own figures and have not been independently sourced.
- No public disclosure gives the Power-only two-year RPO conversion. The inherited Power pre-2025 share (6-16%) could not be re-derived independently in this lane; only the ≤$5.8B cap was.
- The absolute backlog contribution-margin level rests on the analyst's 'about breakeven' framing, which Strazik confirmed only 'directionally'. The consolidated ~19.6%/25.6% levels are sensitive to rounding in the $8B and 6-pt inputs.
- Order year is not the same as pricing year. SRAs priced in late 2024 can convert to orders in 2025, so the booking-year vintages in RPO blur the pricing vintage.
- Net cash for the EV back-solve excludes short-term borrowings, which are not isolated on the Q2'26 balance sheet.
- Paywalled sell-side and Barron's text was not accessed; a rebuttal using RPO may exist there.
- Two corpus metadata errors: the Q4'25 transcript (src:2660) is dated 2026-01-23 but the call was 2026-01-28; src:3771 is labelled 2026-04-22 but is the Q3'25 call of 2025-10-22.

### Corrections to the verified state

- GLJ's premise as stated in the verified state ('2027 ships a 2024-priced book at ~3 points of margin versus 10-11 points on the 2025 vintage') leaves out GLJ's timing. Investing.com and GuruFocus both attach the 10-11 pts to the '2025 vintage shipping in 2028'. GLJ maps 2024 orders to 2027 and 2025 orders to 2028, a 3-year lag. It does not set the 2025 vintage against 2027.
- The 'KNOWN BAD FIGURE' is only half bad. H1'26 operating cash flow of $10,680M IS in the Q2'26 10-Q (0001996810-26-000148), in both the cash flow statement and the FCF reconciliation (p.32). The $14,087M 'increase in deposits' is not found anywhere. The cash-flow line 'Increase (decrease) in contract liabilities and current deferred income' is $13,695M. The balance-sheet change in contract liabilities is $14,170M (25,774 to 39,944).
- Basis note on the segment one-year conversion rates (Power 53.7%, Wind 67.6%, Electrification 36.9%). They reproduce exactly on the MD&A segment equipment revenue, which includes intersegment revenue (6,686 / 7,251 / 7,378). On the external equipment revenue in the segment note (6,420 / 7,224 / 7,290), they are 51.5% / 67.4% / 36.4%. Label the basis.
- Backlog margin definition: GEV's 'margin in backlog' / 'margin expansion' is the change in the average estimated CONTRIBUTION margin of the backlog versus the prior year (4Q'25 slide 5, footnote b). It is not a gross margin and not an EBITDA margin. Any use of GLJ's 3 / 10-11 points as gross-margin or segment EBITDA-margin levels is a definition mismatch.
- The -8.6% day move is confirmed: close $874.85 on 2026-09-14 against $957.27 on 2026-09-11 is -8.61% (GuruFocus). The 24/7 Wall St figure of $873.89 appears to be an intraday price.

---

## RV: Reverse DCF / reverse multiple. What $962.49 requires

**Headline:** Built from the Q2'26 10-Q, the EV at $962.49 is about $250.7B, with a range of $247.1-252.5B depending on bridge conventions. Management's 2028 framework ($11.2B EBITDA) only supports that price at a year-end-2028 exit of roughly 25-28x. Street 2028 EBITDA ($13.06B) needs roughly 22-24x. Any exit multiple of 20x or lower requires 2028 EBITDA above consensus in every discount-rate cell. On the most generous growth case, the physical check needs Power EBITDA of about $290M per GW at 24 GW, against $246M/GW in H1'26 and $312M/GW in Q2'26.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** I checked all 39 facts against the corpus filings and the two live web pages. 29 are verified exactly, including the EV of $250,675M, the whole reverse-multiple grid, the breakeven multiples (framework 25.4-28.3x, consensus 21.8-24.3x) and all the per-GW and $/kW ratios.

One is unverifiable: RV-37, GLJ's 38.9x basis. Its EV and date were not seen, and 38.9x does not reproduce exactly.

Nine need correction or a definition fix:

1. **RV-32 / 'known bad figure' (conclusion change):** the $14,087M is in the 10-Q after all. It equals Note 9's change in total contract liabilities and deferred income (40,037 - 25,950), and the Note 9 text says $14,088 million. It is a balance-sheet change that includes the Prolec acquisition, not an operating-cash line. So the figure is mislabelled, not made up.
2. **RV-17 and grid 2c (conclusion change):** the $24B 2025-2028 FCF target dates from 2026-01-28, when FY26 FCF guidance was $5.0-5.5B. At that time the implied FY27+28 amount was $14.8-15.3B, close to consensus of $16.7B. The low "floor" of $7.8-8.8B exists only because FY26 guidance was later raised and the target was never restated in the Q1 or Q2 releases.
3. **RV-30:** the 60% Power share is measured before Corporate costs but applied to adjusted EBITDA, which is after them. The correct share of $11.2B is 60.6-63.8%, which makes the 25x case $294-309M per GW. The headline also mislabels that cell as "the most generous growth case".
4. **RV-21:** the day count is 824, not 823. The result ($14,542M) is unchanged.
5. **RV-33:** the values are right, but the quoted explanation describes the change in operating cash flow, not the balance. Note 9 attributes the balance increase to customer collections plus the Prolec acquisition.
6. **Pension netting (RV-08):** counting only plans in deficit gives $2,470M, not $1,300M. That adds $1,170M to EV and is a convention for the team to choose.
7. **Share count (RV-36):** the $854 average buyback price needs the exact change in treasury shares (4,266,417). Using the rounded 4.3M gives $848.
8. **MarketScreener EV gap:** it reconciles to about $244.1B once you use its roughly 266M shares and its FY26 forecast net cash of $12,259M. Remove it from the unresolved list.
9. **2028 framework (RV-15):** it was not restated in the Q1 or Q2'26 releases, so cite it as dated 2026-01-28.

Sources: Q2'26 10-Q 0001996810-26-000148, FY25 10-K 0001996810-26-000015, releases 0001996810-26-000012, -000063, -000147 and 0001996810-25-000132, and the Q3'25 and Q1'26 10-Qs, all read from data\gev.db. Web: stockanalysis.com and marketscreener.com, both fetched 2026-09-29.

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- Test 1 (independent re-derivation) is not independent. The closed form is the same equation with FCF = 0, so it checks only the terminal-only cell. I recomputed every cell of grids 2a/2b/2c (2028 and 2030) from scratch: all match within $1M (e.g. 2b 2028 9%/15x 18,974 vs 18,975; 2030 11%/20x 19,539 vs 19,540). Grid arithmetic passes.
- Test 2's 'not reconciled' gap is reconcilable. MarketScreener EV (5.27 x 46,282 = $243.9B) is about 962.49 x ~266.2M shares minus its FY26E net cash of $12,259M, which gives about $244.1B. The gap to $250.7B is share count (-$3.5B, 270M vs ~266M), forward vs 6/30 net cash (-$2.0B: 12,259 vs 13,120-2,849 = 10,271), and NCI + pension - EMI (+$1.07B). Remove the 'not reconciled' item from unresolved.
- Test 3's conclusion is wrong. '$14,087M' is exactly Note 9's change in total contract liabilities and deferred income (40,037-25,950), and Note 9 text gives $14,088M. The KNOWN BAD FIGURE is a definition mismatch (balance change incl. Prolec acquisition and non-current deferred income, mislabelled as an OCF deposits line), not a fabrication. The OCF line is +$13,695M.
- Test 4 is invalid as framed. The $24B cumulative 2025-2028 target was set on 2026-01-28, when FY26 FCF guidance was $5.0-5.5B (implied FY27+28 = $14.8-15.3B, close to consensus $16.7B). FY26 guidance was later raised to $11.5-12.5B without the cumulative being restated in the Q1/Q2'26 releases. The $7.8-8.8B 'floor', the 'consensus ~2x above the floor' reading and grid 2c's 'management-floor FCF' label are artifacts of a stale framework. Do not describe 2c as a management path.
- Test 5 (double count): reasoning holds. 13.1/250.7 = 5.2% checks. Add that about $0.4B of the cash is restricted, and that $39.9B of current contract liabilities against $13.1B of cash means the cash is customer-funded.
- Test 6 (realignment): verified from both releases (+$54M revenue, +$17M EBITDA, 14.0% -> 14.1%).
- Test 7 (physical feasibility) has three problems. (1) The 60% Power share is computed on segment EBITDA before Corporate costs and then applied to adjusted EBITDA. The correct share of $11.2B is 60.6-63.8%, which raises required $/GW by about 1-6% (25x: $294-309M, not $291M). (2) The Q2'26 $312M/GW comparator rests on only 3.3 GW shipped with an aero-heavy mix (16 of 29 units), so it is not a stable benchmark. H1'26 $246M/GW is the better anchor. (3) The headline calls the $290M/GW cell 'the most generous growth case', but it is the 25x / 10% / consensus-FCF / 60% cell. The label is wrong.
- Test 8 ($/kW): the figures are verified. As well as nuclear/hydro/steam, the upper-bound caveat should mention that RPO carries non-turbine scope. The 2026 RPO figures are on the realigned segment basis, so it is not like-for-like with Q3'25/Q4'25.
- Bridge omissions not tested by the researcher: pension netting (deficit-only plans total $2,470M vs net $1,300M; +$1,170M EV), operating leases, Q3 buybacks and dividends after 6/30, and any legacy/indemnity liabilities. The 2028 framework has not been reaffirmed in the Q1/Q2'26 releases, so cite it as dated 2026-01-28.

### Unresolved

- No FY29-FY30 consensus (EBITDA, FCF, EPS) was found on public pages. The 2030 grid uses a flat $9,248M FCF placeholder, and the 2030 EBITDA comparison can only be made against 2028 anchors via implied CAGR.
- FY28 consensus is single-sourced from MarketScreener (EPS $34.22, EBITDA $13,064M). The page shows 'Last Updated January 28, 2026' although its P/E ties to today's price, so the estimate vintage is unclear. stockanalysis FY27-28 is paywalled.
- Power's share of 2028 EBITDA (~60%) is derived from the framework's segment margins and an assumed 16-18% CAGR. Management does not disclose 2028 segment revenue in dollars.
- EBITDA per GW is total Power EBITDA (services ~66% of revenue, plus nuclear, steam, hydro) divided by gas GW shipped. It is a scaling ratio, not unit margin. Services EBITDA grows with the installed base independently of shipments.
- $/kW from Power equipment RPO / firm GW is an upper bound for gas pricing. Nuclear, hydro and steam equipment in RPO cannot be separated out.
- The pension deficit is at YE2025 (not remeasured at Q2'26) and pre-tax. The $516M June contribution is a cash item, not a remeasured funded status.
- Post-6/30 events are not in the bridge: Q3 buybacks, dividends, Robotech acquisition (closed July, size not located), Q3 FCF. The 270M share count is the Q2 weighted diluted figure, not a 9/29 count.
- GLJ's EV and date for its '38.9x forward EV/EBITDA' were not seen. That it matches FY26 rather than FY27 EBITDA is an inference.
- MarketScreener's implied EV (~$243.9B from 5.27x EV/Sales) was not reconciled line by line to our $250.7B bridge.

### Corrections to the verified state

- KNOWN BAD FIGURE partly re-sourced. H1'26 operating cash flow of $10,680M IS in the Q2'26 10-Q (0001996810-26-000148), in the cash flow statement line 'Cash from (used for) operating activities 10,680 1,528'. The '$14,087M increase in deposits' is NOT in the filing. The actual line is 'Increase (decrease) in contract liabilities and current deferred income 13,695', and MD&A gives the year-over-year change as '$11.8 billion'. H1 FCF $9,897M = 10,680 - 783 ties out.
- Diluted weighted-average shares of 270M is the Q2'26 three-month figure. The six-month H1'26 figure is 271M (Note 18). Label the period when using it.
- Power segment margin series: FY23-FY25 are on the pre-2026 segment basis, and H1'26/Q2'26 are on the realigned basis effective 2026-01-01. The recast effect on H1'25 Power is immaterial (+$54M revenue, +$17M EBITDA; 14.0% to 14.1%), but the basis change should be footnoted.
- FY26 consensus EPS: the verified state's '$15.16 ex-Prolec' matches stockanalysis (31 analysts). MarketScreener's FY26 EPS is $30.78, which includes the $3,992M pre-tax Prolec remeasurement gain. This definition difference is also why MarketScreener shows EPS falling from FY26 to FY27 ($24.85).
- The '2028 framework cumulative FCF at least $24B' covers 2025 through 2028 (Q4'25 release p.11), so it includes the 2025 actual of $3,710M. The implied 2027+2028 floor is only $7.8-8.8B, against consensus of $16.7B.

## BX — Bear case and exit multiple anchored on GEV's own drawdowns, its forward-multiple history, and peers

**Headline:** Closing prices from two sources match on all 626 sessions since the spin. The data show five close-to-close drawdowns of 15% or more from a running high (the worst is -38.3%, Jan–Mar 2025) and eight swings of 15% or more when measured peak to trough. GEV's NTM forward P/E since the spin has run from 33.8x to 68.9x, with a median of 50.8x (Chartloom; not point-in-time). Trough multiples fall between 33.8x and 51.1x, and the four 2026 troughs sit at 42.6–47.9x. Today the stock trades at 42.6–46.9x NTM and 38.3–40.0x FY27, which is 2.2–2.4x the S&P 500's 19.2x. On FY27 consensus EPS growth, however, it is priced about in line with Vertiv and Siemens Energy.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** I re-derived all 41 facts from fresh downloads rather than the researcher's cached files: Yahoo, Nasdaq, Chartloom, MarketScreener (via WebFetch), stockanalysis, Nasdaq/Zacks, the FactSet PDFs, Wayback, TIKR, and the corpus 10-Q and 8-K tables. Prices, drawdowns, zigzag swings, event returns, the Chartloom stats, the EV bridge, peer multiples and grid E all reproduce. Results: 34 verified, 4 corrected, 2 definition-mismatch, 0 unverifiable. The corrected rows are minor: BX-01 omits the 2024-04-01 when-issued row that both feeds carry; BX-35's explanation of SA's ABBN forward P/E does not reproduce; BX-37's 7,704.13 is the 09-24 close. The definition-mismatch rows are BX-30 (MHI's current-FY P/E used as FY+1) and BX-39 (the PEG table mixes P/E bases, growth windows and thin FY28 estimates). Four errors change conclusions: (1) The headline says 'four 2026 troughs'; there are three. (2) The Chartloom-vs-point-in-time test has the wrong sign. Chartloom reads high at troughs, so its trough multiples are an upper bound, not a lower one. (3) The multiple-compression decomposition is tautological, so the 'no estimate cuts' conclusion is unsupported. (4) The GLJ 38.9x 'exact' match depends on today's price (38.7x at the pre-initiation price), and the if_long line compares GEV FY27 against peer FY26. Two unresolved items are resolvable: forward EV/EBITDA history is on the Chartloom page (evEbitdaNtm), and the Q2'25 8-K is in the corpus. Separately, the verified-state 'bad figure' H1'26 OCF of $10,680M is in the Q2'26 10-Q cash-flow statement. The $14,087M deposits figure is still unsourced; the nearest line is 13,695. Paths: data\gev.db. My scratch downloads are in <scratch> (files prefixed v_ plus gev.json and spx.json).

### Tests

| test | method | result | if long | if short |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Are GEV closing prices correct? | Downloaded the full daily series from both Yahoo's chart API and Nasdaq's historical API and diffed every date | 626/626 overlapping closes agree within $0.02; Chartloom's embedded price series also matches exactly (0 mismatches) | The drawdown table can be quoted with two sources behind it | Same: the drawdown dates and percentages are not in dispute |
| Is the Chartloom historical forward P/E close enough to point-in-time to use for trough multiples? | Compared Chartloom's implied NTM EPS against three dated consensus snapshots: the Yahoo Wayback capture of 2025-01-15 (FY25 6.68), the 2026-06-22 article (FY26 16.77 / FY27 24.43), and the TIKR point-in-time NTM figure for 2026-09-18 | Jan-2025: 49.4x point-in-time vs 50.0x Chartloom (1% gap). Sep-18-2026: 44.7x vs 45.8x (2% gap). Jun-2026: 43.0x vs 47.9x, where the gap mainly reflects Chartloom's current-quarter-inclusive window. At peaks Chartloom reads lower than TIKR (63.5x vs ~70x spring 2026; 51.3x vs 56.3x 12-month average) because it applies later, higher estimates backward. | Chartloom trough multiples (33.8–51.1x) are usable as a lower-bound reference, with the label 'not point-in-time' | A bear multiple anchored on Chartloom troughs is, if anything, slightly low versus point-in-time; note this when a judge asks |
| Which EBITDA year does GLJ's '38.9x forward EV/EBITDA' use? | Rebuilt EV from the Q2'26 10-Q balance sheet plus the 2026-09-29 price, then divided by MarketScreener consensus EBITDA for each year | EV $247,230M / FY26 $6,359M = 38.9x (exact match); FY27 = 25.8x; at the 09-11 pre-GLJ price, FY26 = 38.7x | GLJ's headline multiple is on the current year. On FY27, GEV is at about 25–26x, below Vertiv's FY26 27.2x and near Quanta's FY26 24.8x | GLJ's case rests on FY27 EBITDA of $7.42B, not on the 38.9x; at $7.42B, FY27 EV/EBITDA is 33.3x (247,230/7,420) |
| How much of each drawdown was multiple compression and how much was estimate change? | Split each peak-to-trough move into the change in Chartloom P/E and the change in implied NTM EPS | Every drawdown inside a calendar quarter is 100% multiple compression in this series, because EPS is fixed within the quarter. The two that crossed a quarter-end (Mar–Apr 2025; Aug–Nov 2025) show P/E falling 35.1% and 33.8% while NTM EPS rose 21.7% and 24.6%. | The history shows the stock can lose 15–38% with no estimate cuts; a bear case can be built on the multiple alone | Estimates rose through every drawdown, so a short needs a catalyst that moves EPS, not only the multiple |
| Were GEV's drawdowns market-driven or stock-specific? | Measured the S&P 500's change over each drawdown window | Only Jan–Apr 2025 coincided with a market drawdown (SPX -18.6% to 04-08). The Aug–Nov 2025, Apr–Jun 2026 and Jun–Sep 2026 drawdowns (-17.5%, -24.6%, -25.5%) happened while SPX rose (+6.7%, +2.2%, +1.6%). | The stock's own drawdown history (15–25% without a market selloff) is the relevant bear anchor | Idiosyncratic volatility is high (daily σ 3.34%, about 53% annualised since the spin) without any fundamental cut |
| Does a guidance raise protect the stock on earnings day? | Close-to-close return on each 8-K Item 2.02 date | 2026-07-22 (raised FY26 revenue to $45.5–46.5B) fell 8.69%; 2026-04-22 rose 13.75%; 2025-07-23 rose 14.58%; 2025-10-22 fell 1.59% | A raise on 2026-10-28 alone may not produce a positive reaction | The last print shows a raise-and-fall pattern at a high multiple |

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- HEADLINE error: 'the four 2026 troughs sit at 42.6-47.9x'. Grid A has only three distinct 2026 trough dates: 06-10 47.9x, 07-29 43.8x, 09-14 42.6x (09-14 appears twice, as 5 and 5b). 'Priced about in line with Vertiv and Siemens Energy' overstates the ENR comparison: 0.65 vs GEV 0.90 is about 28% lower.
- GRID A row 1: the S&P column shows -7.4% (02-10->03-10), but row 1's window is 01-23->03-10, where SPX was -8.24%. The -7.4% belongs to row 1b.
- Chartloom point-in-time test: the conclusion has the wrong sign. In all three checks Chartloom reads HIGHER than point-in-time at or near troughs (50.0 vs 49.4; 47.9 vs 43.0; 45.8 vs 44.7). So Chartloom trough multiples are an UPPER bound, not a lower bound, and a bear multiple anchored on them is if anything generous, not 'slightly low'. Also: the TIKR comparator uses normalized EPS (a different definition), and the June comparator uses estimates dated 12 days after the trough and mixes bases (FY26 adjusted vs a GAAP FY25 base).
- Multiple-vs-estimate decomposition test is tautological. Chartloom holds NTM EPS fixed within each quarter by construction, so '100% multiple compression' inside a quarter is an artifact. Neither 'the stock can lose 15-38% with no estimate cuts' nor 'estimates rose through every drawdown' is supported. Only the two cross-quarter cases show EPS changes (P/E -35.1% / -33.8%, EPS +21.7% / +24.6%, both reproduced), and those are stored snapshots, not dated consensus.
- GLJ 38.9x test: the 'exact match' uses the 09-29 price. GLJ published 09-14 off roughly the 09-11 price, where the same bridge gives 38.7x and MS's EV gives 38.4x. The FY26-not-FY27 conclusion holds (FY27 would be ~25-26x). The if_long line compares GEV FY27 EV/EBITDA (25-26x) with Vertiv FY26 (27.2x) and Quanta FY26 (24.8x), which mixes years. On FY27, VRT is 19.1x and PWR 21.2x, both below GEV.
- Market-driven test: the 'SPX -18.6%' uses a window ending 04-08, past GEV's 03-10 running-high trough. Over the episode window SPX fell -8.2%. Daily sigma is verified (3.35%, 53.1% annualised).
- UNRESOLVED list correction: historical forward EV/EBITDA at the troughs IS on the same Chartloom page (series evEbitdaNtm; same not-point-in-time caveat; EBITDA definition unknown). Values: 2025-01-27 22.84x, 03-10 16.49x, 04-04 15.55x, 11-04 23.38x, 12-17 24.87x, 2026-06-10 28.54x, 07-29 27.54x, 09-14 25.44x, today 27.70x. Peaks: 2025-08-06 33.07x, 2026-04-23 40.39x.
- UNRESOLVED list correction: the Q2'25 release 8-K (0001996810-25-000132, 2025-07-23) is in the corpus filings and documents tables. Its items field is mis-tagged '2.01,9.01'.
- Verified-state 'KNOWN BAD FIGURE' is partly re-sourced. H1'26 operating cash flow of $10,680M IS in the Q2'26 10-Q cash-flow statement ('Cash from (used for) operating activities 10,680 1,528') and in the FCF reconciliation (10,680 - 783 = 9,897). The '$14,087M increase in deposits' is still not found. The nearest line is 'Increase (decrease) in contract liabilities and current deferred income 13,695'.
- Grid D mislabels MHI: its 'FY+1' column shows the current fiscal year FYMar27 at 29.0x. FYMar28 at 25.0x is the comparable figure.
- Grid E (both P/E and EV/EBITDA tables) recomputed cell by cell. All match. Net cash less NCI 9,113 = 13,120 - 2,849 - 1,158.

### Unresolved

- Point-in-time consensus EPS was not found for the 2025-04-04, 2025-11-04, 2025-12-17 and 2026-07-29 troughs. Chartloom values for those dates are hindsight-contaminated (they use stored per-quarter estimates, not the consensus on the trough date).
- Historical forward EV/EBITDA at each trough was not obtained. YCharts and FinanceCharts (captcha) and GuruFocus (JS challenge) were not accessed. MarketScreener gives only current-date EV/EBITDA.
- stockanalysis's year-end forward P/E for GEV (51.958x at 2024-12-31, 59.6x at 2025-12-31) disagrees with Chartloom (49.85x, 42.17x) on the same dates. The implied FY26 EPS at end-2025 is $10.97 vs $15.50. The true point-in-time FY26 consensus at 2025-12-31 was not verified.
- Quanta consensus conflicts between sources: stockanalysis/S&P adjusted FY26 EPS is 16.74 and FY27 19.77; Zacks has 15.37 and 18.02. The PWR growth and PEG figures depend on which source is used.
- ABB FY26 GAAP EPS (3.89) is above FY27 (3.517), which suggests a one-off gain in FY26. The item was not identified from ABB filings.
- Peer EBITDA and EV/EBITDA come from MarketScreener only; no second source for forward EBITDA was found for any peer. MarketScreener's EBITDA definition for peers was not checked against each company's adjusted-EBITDA definition.
- European 2026-09-29 closes (ENR, ABBN, SU) are missing from Yahoo; the figures rely on MarketScreener's 09-29 last price.
- GEV's FY25 adjusted EPS of $16.67 (stockanalysis) includes Q4'25 GAAP EPS of $13.36, which appears to carry a large tax item. A FY25A→FY27E growth rate for GEV is therefore distorted; the tables use FY26E→FY28E (Zacks) instead.
- The 8-K for the Q2'25 release (2025-07-23; accession 0001996810-25-000132 per sec.gov search result) is missing from the corpus filings table; the date was taken from the search result, not the corpus.

### Corrections to the verified state

- The late-April to late-September 2026 decline is -16.27% close to close (2026-04-23 $1,149.53 to 2026-09-29 $962.49), not ~15%. To the 2026-09-14 trough it is -23.90%. The larger open episode runs from the all-time closing high on 2026-06-30 ($1,174.86): -25.54% to 09-14 and -18.08% to 09-29.
- GLJ's '38.9x forward EV/EBITDA' reproduces exactly as EV / FY2026 consensus EBITDA ($247,230M / $6,359M). It is not on FY2027, where GEV trades at 25.0–25.8x. Any memo line that compares 38.9x with FY27 figures mixes years.
- The 52-week range $530.16–$1,195.94 is intraday (low 2025-11-21, high 2026-07-06). On closes, the 52-week range is $547.96 (2025-11-04) to $1,174.86 (2026-06-30).
- Clarification, not an error: 'FY26 EPS ex-Prolec $15.16' is stockanalysis's adjustedEps field. FY26 GAAP consensus is $30.78 (MarketScreener/stockanalysis 'eps'), inflated by the $4,428M H1'26 gain on business interests (Q2'26 10-Q cash-flow statement). MarketScreener's FY26 P/E of 31.3x is on GAAP and should not be used.

## BR: beat-and-raise record (guidance history, raise pace, 2028 framework vs consensus, raise-day stock reactions)

**Headline:** At company level, GEV's beat-and-raise record is mainly about FCF and revenue, not margin. Its first revenue guide for each year was beaten by only +1.3% (FY24) and +4.3% (FY25). Adj EBITDA margin finished slightly BELOW the first numeric midpoint in both years. FCF beat by 65-89%. To reach consensus FY28, the 2028 framework would need a +8.0% revenue raise plus +1.6pt of margin. That is bigger than any beat on a year's first guide, but about half of the one organic framework raise (Dec-2025: +15.6% revenue, +6pt margin). Three of seven raise days closed lower, including Q2'26 at -8.69%.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** I checked all 44 facts against corpus filings, the two December investor-update PDFs, the GE and ge.com sources, the GEV IR events page, and the corpus prices table, which runs to 2026-09-18 rather than being stale at 2025-09-18. 38 verified, 4 corrected, 2 definition-mismatch.

Corrections:
- BR-36 and BR-38: the 2024-12-10 update was released at 4:05 PM ET, after the close, so its reaction day is 12-11 at +5.01%, not 12-10 at -2.34%. The stock fell on 2 of 7 raise days, not 3. Mean +5.51%, median +5.01%.
- BR-20: the conclusion holds, but the locator is wrong. The 2028 hits are capex $6B plus R&D $5B in both releases, and 24 GW only in Q2'26.

Definition mismatches:
- BR-23: the margin 'first numeric' baselines (6.0%, 8.5%) are mid-year raised guides, not initial guides. So the claim that margin was never beaten is overstated.
- BR-31: FY27 growth of +14.65% is measured from the guide midpoint. Against consensus FY26 it is +13.95%.

Test errors:
- The '6-14% too high if 20% holds' range does not match Grid A (9.3-14.3%).
- Calling Dec-2025 the 'only organic framework raise' misses Dec-2024 (+4pt).
- 'About half' of the Dec-2025 raise is true for revenue only; for margin it is about a quarter.
- The Q3 ~13% pass mark matches the low end of guidance, not the midpoint (15.0%).

Confirmed:
- All guidance quotes.
- FY24 and FY25 actuals.
- H1'26 CFOA $10,680M and contract liabilities +$13,695M. There is no $14,087M line.
- Prolec accounts for the whole Jan-2026 revenue step.
- No Dec-2026 event is listed; Q3 earnings are on 2026-10-28.

Q2'25 release: '8%-9%, raising the low end of the previous guidance of high-single digits'. Dec-24 PR: 'High-single digits, up from the low end of high-single digits'. Revenue is compared with the year's first guide, but margin with a mid-year raise, so the two baselines differ. |

### Tests

| test | method | result | if long | if short |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Is 'raised or reaffirmed, never cut' true at every level? | Went through every guidance sentence in 12 events (BR-01 to BR-19) at company and segment level | Company-level revenue and FCF were never lowered. But the FY24 adj EBITDA margin midpoint fell 25bp when the range was narrowed in Dec-2024 (BR-05). Wind was cut at Q3'25 (BR-11) and its 2028 margin was cut from 10% to 6% (BR-13). Wind also missed its final guide in FY24 (BR-43) and FY25 (BR-42). | The company-level record holds for revenue and FCF. Describe it precisely ('company-level revenue/FCF') so a judge cannot cite the Dec-24 margin narrowing or the Wind cuts against it. | The segment record shows Wind guidance is unreliable. H1'26 Wind is already -$657M against a ~-$400M FY guide (BR-34). |
| Where does the beat come from: revenue, margin or cash? | Compared each year's first numeric midpoint with the actual (BR-21 to BR-24) | Revenue +1.3% (FY24) and +4.3% (FY25). Adj EBITDA margin -0.17pt and -0.10pt, i.e. below the first numeric midpoint both years. FCF +89% and +65%, but FY25 FCF finished 1.1% below its final midpoint. | Consensus FY28 needs margin above the framework (21.6% vs 20%). The annual-guide record gives no precedent for margin beats; the framework raises (+4pt, +6pt) are the only precedent. | A thesis that 'the Street is above management' rests on margin, the one metric GEV has not beaten at annual level. |
| What 2028 raise pace does consensus assume, compared with history? | Applied observed raise rates to the $56B / 20% framework (grid below) | Consensus needs +8.04% revenue and +1.59pt margin. That exceeds every annual first-guide revenue beat (max +5.74%) but is about half the one organic framework revision (+15.56% revenue, +6pt margin, Dec-2025). At +4.3% revenue and 20% margin, 2028 EBITDA lands at $11.68B, 10.6% below consensus. | Consensus is roughly one more December framework raise at half the Dec-2025 size. On the 2024 and 2025 pattern, that event falls inside the 3-12 month horizon (BR-44). | If the December update holds 20% or raises revenue only at the annual-beat pace, Street FY28 EBITDA is 6-14% too high. |
| Does the stock fall on raises? | Close-to-close on the reaction day vs SPY for all 12 guidance events (BR-36, BR-38, BR-39) | 3 of 7 raise days fell (-4.45%, -2.34%, -8.69%). The largest fall was Q2'26, when revenue and FCF were raised but the margin guide was held and GAAP EPS missed. All 5 reaffirm days were within -1.6% to +3.1%. | Big up-moves (+13.75%, +14.58%, +15.62%) came when margin or the multi-year framework was raised, not revenue or FCF alone. | The Jul-2026 raise that failed shows the mechanism: at ~2x market multiple, a revenue/FCF raise without a margin raise gave -8.69%. |
| Has the 2028 framework gone stale against the 2026 run-rate? | Compared the unchanged 2028 FCF framework with actuals plus the FY26 guide (BR-20, BR-32) | FY25 actual plus the FY26 midpoint equals $15.71B, 65.5% of the at-least-$24B cumulative target. That leaves $8.29B for 2027-28 combined, while consensus has $16.67B. The FY26 FCF guide is up +129% since the framework was last set (5.25 to 12.0). | FCF is the metric most likely to be raised at a December event. | The FY26 FCF raise is deposit-driven (contract liabilities +$13,695M in H1, BR-40). Pull-forward means that same deposit cash is not repeated in 2027-28. |
| What does the FY26 guide require in H2 (Oct-28 check)? | FY guide minus H1 actuals (BR-33, BR-34, BR-35) | The midpoint needs a 15.0% H2 adj EBITDA margin vs 10.5% in H1 (13.2% at the low end). Wind needs about +$257M in H2. Power needs H2 organic growth well above 20%. | If Q3 prints a margin at or above ~13%, that shows the midpoint is on track. | H2 is heavily back-end loaded. Prior Q3s were reaffirm-only (BR-39). |

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- Headline error: 'Three of seven raise days closed lower' is wrong. The Dec-2024 update was released 4:05 PM ET on 12-10, so its reaction day is 12-11 (+5.01%). The corrected count is 2 of 7, mean +5.51%, median +5.01%. Grid C's Dec-2024 row should read +5.01% (SPY +0.77%), with 12-10 at -2.34% shown only as the pre-release session.
- 'Where does the beat come from' test mixes baselines. Revenue is measured against each year's first guide; margin is measured against the first NUMERIC guide, which in both years was itself a mid-year raise (5-7% on 2024-07-24; 8-9% on 2025-07-23). Against the initial qualitative guides, margin met or beat ('MSD toward higher end' -> 5.83%; 'low end of HSD' -> 8.40%). The if_short line ('margin, the one metric GEV has not beaten') and the headline's margin sentence are overstated. They should say 'finished marginally below the mid-year raised numeric midpoint'.
- 2028 raise-pace test: calling Dec-2025 'the one organic framework raise' is wrong. Dec-2024 was also an organic framework raise (margin 10%->14%, +4pt; growth MSD->HSD). It just has no dollar revenue baseline. Also, consensus needs roughly HALF the Dec-25 revenue raise (8.04/15.56=0.52) but only about a QUARTER of its margin raise (1.59/6=0.27), so 'about half' applies to revenue only.
- 2028 raise-pace test: 'If the December update holds 20% ... Street FY28 EBITDA is 6-14% too high' does not match Grid A. At a 20% margin with 0% to +5.74% revenue raises, the gap is 9.3-14.3%. The 6% bound needs a 21% margin (the +4.30% row gives -6.1%), which contradicts 'holds 20%'.
- 2028 raise-pace test: comparing a multi-year framework revision (+8.04% on 2028) with one-year first-guide beats compares different horizons. The if_long line ('that event falls inside the 3-12 month horizon') is conditional: no Dec-2026 event is announced (IR page checked 2026-09-29). The Q3'26 release on 2026-10-28 is where prior years pre-announced it.
- Stock-reaction test: the if_long claim that big up-moves came when margin or the framework was raised has a counterexample. Q2'24 raised the margin guide (MSD high end -> 5-7%) and the stock fell 4.45%, on a day SPY fell 2.27% (relative -2.18pt). The Dec-24 framework raise (corrected +5.01%) was modest. With n=7 and no market adjustment beyond SPY, the pattern is anecdotal. The Q2'26 -8.69% also coincided with a GAAP EPS miss of about 19% vs press consensus, so it cannot be attributed to the held margin guide alone.
- H2 test: 'If Q3 prints a margin at or above ~13%, that shows the midpoint is on track' is wrong. 13.2% is the H2 average needed for the LOW end. The midpoint needs a 15.0% H2 average, so a 13% Q3 implies Q4 of about 17% for the midpoint. The pass mark should be labelled low-end.
- H2 test: 'Power needs H2 organic growth well above 20%' is stated as a result, but the researcher's own unresolved list says it cannot be computed without the recast FY25 Power base. Treat it as plausible but unverified.
- Framework-stale test: the arithmetic checks out ($8.29B left vs consensus $16.67B; FY26 FCF guide +128.6% since Jan). The deposit-driven attribution is supported by the 10-Q MD&A ('$13.7 billion, driven by down payments on orders and slot reservation agreements').
- 'Never cut' test: supported, and it could add the Q2'25 Wind 'trending toward the bottom of the range' as an earlier soft cut. The BR-05 narrowing raised the low end (+0.5pt) while cutting the top (-1pt), so describe it as a narrowing with a 25bp lower midpoint, not a plain cut.
- Brief correction: the corpus prices table is NOT stale at 2025-09-18. GEV and SPY rows run to 2026-09-18, and they independently confirm every 2025-10 to 2026-07 event close. This resolves the single-sourcing concern in unresolved item 5.
- Grid A label '+3.37% (... median)' is not the median of the listed annual beats (1.26/3.37/4.30/5.74 gives a median of 3.84). Relabel the row. The cell arithmetic spot-checks correctly (e.g. 60,502x21.59%=13,062; 64,714x26%=16,826).

### Unresolved

- 2028 segment margin targets from the Mar-2024 investor day: the Dec-2024 PR gives Power 16%, Wind 10%, Electrification 16% with no 'up from' values, so the Mar-2024 segment targets are unverified.
- Mar-2024 investor day press release: the web version shows no guidance tables. The 2025 figures from Mar-2024 (MSD organic growth, low end of HSD margin, $1.2-1.8B FCF) come from the 'up from' language in the Dec-2024 PR, not from the Mar-2024 document.
- Timing of the Dec-10-2024 investor update (before or after the open) is unverified. Both the event-day (-2.34%) and next-day (+5.01%) returns are reported.
- Dec-2025 event timing: the reaction shows on Dec-10. Whether the release came after the Dec-9 close is not confirmed.
- Stock returns for 2025-10 onward come from Yahoo history via a fetch tool, cross-checked by stockanalysis (Jul-2026 and Sep-2026 rows) and Investing.com (Jul-21 close). Oct-22-2025, Dec-2025, Jan-28 and Apr-22-2026 closes are single-sourced; Oct and Apr are consistent with press-reported -1.6% and ~14%. The 2024-25 closes come from the corpus prices table and were not re-checked externally.
- How much of the Jan-2026 cumulative FCF raise ($22B to $24B) comes from Prolec vs organic is not disclosed.
- Whether MarketScreener's FY28 'EBITDA' ($13,064M) is defined the same way as GEV adjusted EBITDA is unverified. A definition gap would shift Grid A.
- Power H2'26 growth needed to hit 18-20% can't be computed exactly without the recast FY25 Power revenue (segments were realigned effective 2026-01-01).
- Whether a Dec-2026 investor update will be held (and so the next 2028 framework revision) is not announced as of 2026-09-29.
- The Q2'26 EPS consensus (~$3.04) is press-cited, and some outlets label $2.47 as 'adjusted'. GEV reports only GAAP diluted EPS in 2026.

### Corrections to the verified state

- 'KNOWN BAD FIGURE' is partly wrong. H1'26 operating cash flow of $10,680M IS in the Q2'26 10-Q (0001996810-26-000148), both in the cash flow statement and in the FCF reconciliation on p.32. The deposit-type line is 'Increase (decrease) in contract liabilities and current deferred income 13,695'. The $14,087M figure does not appear; use $13,695M.
- 'Raised or reaffirmed at every report, never cut' holds only for company-level revenue and FCF. The Dec-2024 update narrowed FY24 adj EBITDA margin to 5.5-6% from 5-7%, lowering the midpoint 25bp. Wind segment guidance was cut at Q3'25, and the Wind 2028 margin was cut from 10% to 6% at Dec-2025. Wind missed its final guide in FY24 (43% vs 'nearly 50%') and FY25 (-$598M vs ~-$400M).
- The 2026 revenue step from $41-42B to $44-45B (2026-01-28) and the 2028 step from $52B to $56B are Prolec inclusion (~$3B in 2026, ~$4B in 2028), not organic raises. The Dec-2025 guide explicitly excluded Prolec. Ex-Prolec, the FY26 revenue guide is up only +3.37% cumulative, and 2028 organic revenue is unchanged at $52B.
- 'No FY2027 guidance; expected at the January 2027 call': the first numeric FY2025 and FY2026 guides were both issued at December investor updates (2024-12-10, 2025-12-09), not at January calls. On that pattern, FY2027 guidance and a 2028 revision may come in December 2026 if an event is held (not yet announced).
- Power margin series mixes bases: FY23-FY25 figures are pre-realignment, while H1'26 (17.63%) is after the 2026-01-01 segment realignment. The recast H1'25 Power margin is 14.1% (Q2'26 release six-months table).
- The Dec-2025 update also raised FY2025 FCF guidance to $3.5-4.0B. FY25 actual FCF of $3,710M is below that final midpoint.

---

## CT — Consensus table (FY2026–FY2028), price targets, short interest

**Headline:** You can defend consensus line by line, but only after splitting it by EPS basis and by vintage. FY26 EPS exists on two bases about $15 apart: GAAP including the non-taxable $3,992M Prolec gain, near $30.7, and ex-items, near $15.2–15.6. GEV itself publishes no adjusted EPS. The FY28 EPS gap ($34.22 vs ~$30.52) comes from vintage, not basis: current FactSet and S&P sit at $34.2–34.8, and ~$30.2–30.5 was the February 2026 figure. Only MarketScreener has FY27 and FY28 FCF. No free annual segment consensus exists.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** All 39 facts checked: 27 verified, 5 corrected, 5 unverifiable, 2 definition-mismatch. All the core MarketScreener consensus rows reproduce exactly on 9-29 (CT-01/02/04/05/06), and the 10-Q/10-K figures for EPS, shares, cash from operations (CFOA) and tax verify. So do the 2028 framework, the GLJ quotes and the S&P target panel.

Corrections: (1) CT-20/22 omit an $86M Financial Services gain and use Q1 China XD (+251) instead of H1 (+264). Proficy's after-tax $210M IS disclosed. The corrected other items are $574M = $2.12/share, the ex-all range is ~$13.9-15.2, and the conclusion still holds. (2) Equibles now shows a 9-23 snapshot at 30.78 / 24.85, identical to MarketScreener. (3) WallStreetZen now shows 18.17 / 24.91 / 34.80. (4) The Visible Alpha 'after Q2' timing is inferred; the article says only 'recent results'. (5) One cnyes date is 4-25, not 4-24; the GLJ article date shows 9-15.

Definition mismatches: the FactSet FY25 '$7.28 actual' is unverified and appears to be a pre-report estimate, and CT-24's 'consistent-basis' growth series mixes three vendors.

Short interest: the +10.3% is monthly; the latest semi-monthly change was -0.07%.

Unverifiable because the sites were unreachable: Yahoo, MarketWatch, Barchart, FindBillion and the CNBC estimates.

Key sources: data\gev.db (accessions 0001996810-26-000148, -26-000064, -26-000015, -26-000147, -26-000012). The Laguna transcript PDF was saved by the fetch tool to <session-files>

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- EBITDA-basis test: the conclusion holds (EBITDA actuals 2,035 / 3,196 = 10-K Adjusted EBITDA). But MarketScreener's EBIT actuals (471 / 1,388) are GAAP operating income, so the page mixes bases. Never derive D&A or an EBIT margin from EBITDA minus EBIT on that page.
- FY26 EPS bridge: the item list is incomplete. China XD for H1 is +264 (not +251), and a +86 pre-tax Financial Services equity-method sale gain was missed. Proficy's tax IS disclosed ($210M after-tax), so 'tax effect not disclosed' is wrong for Proficy. Corrected other items: 574 pre-tax = $2.12/share; ex-all ≈ $13.9-15.2. The Q2 Prolec purchase-price-allocation tax true-up (Q2 ETR 29.8%) adds unquantified noise. The reconciliation still closes within ~$1 of the 15.15-15.57 ex-items consensus, so the qualitative conclusion stands.
- FY28 vintage test: sound in direction. But WallStreetZen is now 34.80 (not 34.93), FindBillion is unverifiable (403), and the MarketWatch 22.45-46.41 range is unverifiable. Use the cnyes/FactSet 7-23 range of 21.5-44.96 (2.09x) instead. The ~$30.5 attribution is bracketed only by the 2-9 (30.18) and 4-23 (32.63) FactSet points, so it is an inference.
- Consistent-basis growth (CT-24) is not on one basis: FactSet FY25 7.28 (unverified pre-report estimate), S&P FY26 15.16 (S&P's own FY25 base is 16.67) and MarketScreener GAAP-type FY27/28. Label it cross-vendor, or drop it.
- Source-independence test: the identical $46,282M revenue across MarketScreener, stockanalysis and Equibles supports a shared revenue feed. EPS, however, diverges between stockanalysis/S&P (15.16) and MarketScreener/Equibles (30.78), so the 'single feed' claim holds for revenue, not for EPS. The Yahoo figures could not be re-fetched.
- Guidance test: arithmetic verified. Add the top-of-guide H2 margin (16.75%) as the other boundary: consensus (16.30%) sits between guide-mid (15.00%) and guide-top.
- GLJ 38.9x test: reconciles at the 9-29 EV. GLJ's own price basis was ~$957 (prior close before 9-14; $470 was '51% below prior closing'), within 0.6% of 9-29, so the match is not a coincidence of dates. GLJ's FY26 EBITDA input is still unpublished, so the result is 'consistent with FY26', not 'proven FY26'.
- Short-interest test: '+10.3% month on month' is correct against stockanalysis's prior-month 7.94M. MarketBeat's prior semi-monthly figure is 8,763,426, so short interest was flat (-0.07%) in the period ending 9-15. Do not describe it as rising into or after the GLJ call.
- Rating-count test: holds. The S&P panel low is $940, which excludes GLJ. MarketBeat's MS 'Underweight' conflicts with TipRanks/stockanalysis 'Buy $1,350' and with Arcaro hosting management at Laguna on 9-16. Treat it as unconfirmed.
- Unreachable in this pass (treat as unverified until re-fetched in a browser): Yahoo analysis tables (CT-07/08), MarketWatch (CT-12, the FY25 7.28 and the FY28 34.76), Barchart (CT-14), FindBillion (CT-15), CNBC estimates (CT-26 quote, CT-32).

### Unresolved

- FY27 and FY28 FCF consensus ($7,417M / $9,248M) come only from MarketScreener. No second free source was found. Visible Alpha via Motley Fool gives FY26 $12.4B and FY29 $10.9B only.
- Analyst counts per year for FY28 (and for MarketScreener's EBITDA and FCF lines) are not disclosed on any free page found.
- No free annual segment consensus (Power, Electrification, Wind revenue or margin, 2026–2028) was found. The only Power 2027 margin figure (20.7%) is GLJ's own characterisation.
- The exact date of the Yahoo Finance snapshot is unverified (cached page showing an intraday price of $944.66). Refetch in a browser before citing its analyst counts.
- Morgan Stanley's current rating conflicts between sources: MarketBeat 'Underweight' 8/3 vs TipRanks/stockanalysis 'Buy $1,350' 9/17. Confirm on a primary feed.
- The tax effect of the Proficy ($330M) and China XD gains is not disclosed, so the GAAP-to-ex-items FY26 bridge is approximate (±~$1/share).
- No current source for the ~$30.52 FY28 EPS was found. Its origin is inferred from the FactSet time series (Feb 2026 mean $30.18), not confirmed.
- Zacks-branded FY26 EPS appears on both bases ($30.62 in the 9-3 Zacks article; $15.57 on Barchart's Zacks feed). Which one Zacks' own consensus page uses is unresolved: Zacks blocked the fetch.
- The Hazeltree 'most-shorted large NA stock, tenth consecutive month' claim (via TIKR) was not verified and uses a different measure from exchange short interest.

### Corrections to the verified state

- KNOWN BAD FIGURE, partly re-sourced. H1'26 operating cash flow of $10,680M IS in the Q2'26 10-Q (0001996810-26-000148): it is in the cash flow statement and in the FCF reconciliation on p.32, and also in the Q2 release. The '$14,087M increase in deposits' is not a figure in the filing. The operating cash flow line 'Increase in contract liabilities and current deferred income' is $13,695M. The MD&A says contract liabilities and deferred income rose $14,088M, 'primarily due to new collections … and as a result of the acquisition of Prolec GE'. The balance-sheet change is +$14,170M (25,774 to 39,944).
- Diluted weighted-average shares of 270M apply to Q2'26 (three months). The six-month H1'26 figure is 271M (Q2'26 10-Q Note 18: 'Diluted weighted-average shares outstanding 270 276 271 278').
- The '$15.16 ex-Prolec' FY26 EPS label is imprecise. It is S&P Global's ex-items basis (stockanalysis, range $12.42–18.15, 31 analysts). GEV publishes no adjusted EPS, so the definition is set by each aggregator. MarketScreener's own FY26 EPS is $30.78, on the GAAP basis including the gain.
- The earlier MARKET_BRIEFING row 'Adj. EPS $15.21 | $24.85–25.31 | $34.22' mixes bases. MarketScreener's EPS row, the source of $24.85 and $34.22, has a GAAP-type FY25 (17.69) and FY26 (30.78). FY27 and FY28 are not affected in practice, because no one-time items are known for those years.
- The FY28 EPS contradiction is a matter of vintage, not basis. Current FY28 consensus is $34.22–34.93 across MarketScreener, FactSet ($34.71 mean on 7-23; $34.76 via MarketWatch on 9-2), FindBillion and WallStreetZen. FactSet's FY28 mean was $30.18 on 2026-02-09, so ~$30.52 is a stale early-2026 figure.
- '0 Sell' is specific to the S&P Global panel, which excludes GLJ. TipRanks, MarketWatch/FactSet and MarketBeat each count at least 1 Sell (GLJ $470).
- GLJ's 'Street FY27 EBITDA ~$9.45B' is the mid-September figure. MarketScreener showed $9,574M on 2026-09-29, so GLJ's $7,420M is 22.5% below the current number (21.5% below GLJ's quoted $9.45B).
- FY26 revenue 'corroborated' needs a qualifier. MarketScreener, stockanalysis, Equibles and Yahoo show identical means ($46,282M), which points to one feed. The independent check is FactSet (via cnyes, 7-23: mean $45,913M, median $46,062M, before post-Q2 revisions).

## CQ: cash quality and EV-to-per-share bridge

**Headline:** Customer deposits explain all of GEV's reported operating cash since FY2023. The change in contract liabilities (customer deposits) exceeded total OCF in FY23, FY24, FY25 and H1'26. OCF excluding that line was -$1,626M, -$216M, -$3,032M and -$3,015M. The unsourced "$14,087M" is the Note 9 balance change in total contract liabilities, which includes Prolec and non-current items. It is not the cash-flow line, which is $13,695M. Bridge inputs at 6/30/26: cash $13,120M, borrowings $2,849M, NCI $1,158M, net pension deficit about $1,300M (at 12/31/25). SBC is $257M in FY25 and $135M in H1'26, and it is NOT added back in adjusted EBITDA.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** I checked all 44 facts against the corpus filings, the 07-22 call transcript chunks and MarketScreener. 38 verified (CQ-07 and CQ-12 carry $1M rounding notes), 4 corrected, 1 definition-mismatch and 1 unverifiable.

Material issues:
(1) CQ-26 / corrections list: the researcher's 'correction' of the H1 buyback average to $847.67 is wrong. The Q1'26 10-Q table gives 1,799K Q1 shares, so H1 = 4,266K shares at $854.43, and the original ~$854 stands.
(2) CQ-20: adj EBITDA does add back some SBC. The separation-cost add-back includes a 'one-time stock option grant' (≤$180M FY25, ≤$61M H1'26; exact amount not disclosed). SBC embedded in adj EBITDA is a range, not a flat 8.0% / 6.3%.
(3) CQ-17: the 6/30/26 numerator (38,064) includes current deferred income, while the 10-K figures (15,511; 23,524) exclude it. On a consistent basis the series is 36.7% / 37.0% / 43.3%, and SRA deposits have no matching RPO.
(4) CQ-34: finance leases are ~$297M (2,849 - 2,552), not $295M.
(5) Test 4 overstates the deposit growth consensus needs, because it anchors on history and ignores the tripling of EBITDA. The required ΔCL is closer to ~+$5B than +$8.5-11.7B.
(6) FY23 is pre-spin, so 'since the spin' is inaccurate.

Confirmed: the core resolution of the KNOWN BAD figure. $14,087M is the Note 9 total change; the OCF line is $13,695M; OCF $10,680M is correct. Also confirmed: all cash-flow lines, the Q2 standalone ties, the bridge inputs (cash 13,120; debt 2,849; NCI 1,158; leases 871; pension -1,300 at 12/31/25; EMI 1,384), the EV and multiple arithmetic, the MarketScreener values including the net-debt = negative gross cash finding, and the FCF guide path.

### Tests

| test | method | result | if long | if short |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Is the '$14,087M increase in deposits inside OCF' claim correct? | Read the Q2'26 10-Q cash flow statement and Note 9 and compare definitions | The number exists but carries the wrong label. $14,087M = 40,037-25,950 is the Note 9 change in TOTAL contract liabilities, including non-current items and Prolec's acquired balances; the text says $14,088M. The cash-flow line inside the $10,680M OCF is $13,695M. OCF $10,680M is correct. | Use $13,695M. Using $14,087M overstates deposit dependence by ~$392M, and a judge who reads the 10-Q will catch it. | Same correction. The deposit-dependence point stands on $13,695M anyway. |
| Can the Q2'26 standalone numbers be reproduced two ways? | H1 10-Q minus Q1 10-Q, compared with the CFO's call statements | Q2 FCF $5,106M vs 'about $5.1 billion'; Q2 working capital +$6,389M vs 'a $6.4 billion cash benefit'. H1 working capital +$11,699M vs MD&A '$11.7 billion'. All tie. | The cash figures are internally consistent. There is no restatement or classification issue. | There is no accounting 'gotcha' in the cash-flow numbers. The argument has to be about timing (deposit reversal), not quality of reporting. |
| Did operating cash flow rely on deposits every year? | OCF minus ΔCL, and FCF minus ΔCL, for FY23, FY24, FY25, H1'25 and H1'26 | OCF ex ΔCL was negative in every period: -1,626 / -216 / -3,032 / -332 / -3,015. FCF ex ΔCL: -2,370 / -1,099 / -4,309 / -3,798. Caveat: excluding ALL working capital gives OCF of +56 / +1,464 / +890 / -1,019. Deposits fund inventory and receivables build, so the ΔCL-only cut is the harshest framing. | Expect this Q&A attack. The answer is the all-working-capital cut (CQ-15) plus the rise in deposit intensity (CQ-17). Also give the one-offs in H1'26: $516M pension contribution and $1,258M cash taxes. | Reported cash does not show the business funding itself before customer money in any period since the spin. The judge-proof version is CQ-13 with the CQ-15 caveat stated. |
| What does consensus FY27 FCF of $7,417M imply about deposits? | FY27 FCF = (FCF excluding ΔCL) + ΔCL. Solve for the ΔCL needed to hit $7,417M across a range of FCF-ex-ΔCL values. ### Verifier's audit of the tests

- Test 1 ($14,087M): the conclusion is correct. One wording fix: the $392M excess over the CF line is not caused by 'non-current items', because non-current deferred income fell $82M. It comes from Prolec acquired balances, FX and other non-cash items.
- Test 2 (Q2 standalone): the method and ties are correct. Note that the release prints Q2 FCF as $5,107M, not the computed $5,106M.
- Test 3 (OCF ex ΔCL): the arithmetic is correct. Two corrections to the wording. (a) 'Since the spin' / 'since FY2023' is wrong: FY23 and Q1'24 are pre-spin carve-out periods (spin 2024-04-02). (b) The CF line includes CSA liabilities and deferred income, not only customer deposits; 'deposits' is a simplification. The headline 'deposits explain all of reported OCF' holds only on the ΔCL-only cut. On the all-WC cut (CQ-15), OCF ex WC was positive in FY23-FY25.
- Test 4 (FY27 FCF grid): this is the most important flaw. The algebra is correct, but anchoring FY27 'FCF ex ΔCL' on FY23-H1'26 history ignores that consensus FY27 adj EBITDA ($9,574M) is about 3x FY25 ($3,196M). Holding the FY25 structure constant, +$6.4B of EBITDA alone moves FCF ex ΔCL from -4.3B to about +2.1B before higher cash taxes. The required ΔCL falls to roughly +$5B, not +$8.5-11.7B. The conclusion 'consensus embeds continued deposit inflow' probably still holds, but the magnitude is overstated. The grid also treats FCF ex ΔCL and ΔCL as independent. In a deposit reversal, inventory and receivables build usually unwinds at the same time, so the reversal columns overstate the downside. Consensus FY27 FCF is single-sourced (MarketScreener).
- Test 5 (SBC): the claim 'adj EBITDA does NOT add SBC back' is incomplete. The separation-cost add-back explicitly includes a 'one-time stock option grant' (≤$180M FY25, ≤$61M H1'26; exact amount not disclosed). The embedded SBC share is a range, not 8.0% and 6.3%. The 'SBC is a non-issue' framing needs that caveat.
- Test 6 (bridge): the researcher's 'correction' of the buyback average price is itself WRONG. The Q1'26 10-Q discloses 1,799K Q1 shares, so H1 = 4,266K and the average is $854.43. The verified state (~$854) was right. Finance leases are ~$297M, not $295M. The other bridge inputs hold.
- Test 7 (GLJ 38.9x): the inference that 38.9x is an FY26/NTM multiple rests on the coincidence with 38.7x at a different price. GLJ published on 2026-09-14 at a price below $962.49 (the stock fell 8.6% that day), which would lower any multiple. The attribution to FY26 remains unverified.
- Grid B: arithmetic verified (256,058/26.7x; 266,044/27.8x; 286,016/29.9x). The method treats CL as debt while keeping the cash they funded in net cash. That is a valid sensitivity, but it is not symmetric unless the associated inventory and contract assets are also considered.
- CQ-17: the reported deposit-intensity ratios mix definitions across dates, and the SRA down payments in the numerator have no RPO in the denominator.

### Unresolved

- Mid-2026 pension funded status is not disclosed in the 10-Q; the only figure is 12/31/25 (-$1,300M, pre-tax). The effect of the $516M contribution plus any remeasurement is unknown until the FY26 10-K.
- The $369M gap between Prolec cash consideration ($5,254M) and net cash paid ($4,885M) is presumably cash acquired. Prolec's cash balance at acquisition is not separately disclosed in the text reviewed.
- The exact split of the $475M gap between the balance-sheet CL change (+14,170) and the cash-flow line (+13,695) into Prolec acquired CL, FX and reclasses is not disclosed. Note 9 says only that Prolec contributed.
- Exact H1'26 cash paid for tax withholding on equity awards is not disclosed as a line item. The $503M equity-statement debit is net of option proceeds, and MD&A gives only the y/y change (+$0.3B).
- Q3'26 buybacks and share count after 6/30 are unknown until the 10-Q expected ~2026-10-28.
- The consensus FY27 FCF of $7,417M is single-sourced (MarketScreener, fetched 2026-09-29) and not corroborated elsewhere. The analyst count behind it is unknown.
- GLJ's 38.9x forward EV/EBITDA basis (year, price, EV inputs) is unverified.
- No FY27 FCF guidance exists. Management said only that H2'26 FCF will be 'substantially' below H1.

### Corrections to the verified state

- KNOWN BAD FIGURE resolved. $14,087M is the Note 9 change in TOTAL contract liabilities and deferred income (40,037 - 25,950; the filing text says $14,088M). That total includes non-current deferred income and Prolec acquired balances. The increase inside H1'26 OCF of $10,680M is $13,695M (cash flow statement p.8). OCF $10,680M itself is correct.
- Diluted weighted-average shares: 270M is the Q2'26 three-month figure; the H1'26 six-month figure is 271M (Note 18).
- The H1'26 average buyback price computes to $847.67 ($3,645M / 4.3M shares, with the share count rounded; range $838-858), not ~$854. Q2 alone was 2.467M shares at $952.47.
- FY24 FCF: the computed figure is $1,700M (2,583 - 883); the Q4'25 release and MarketScreener show $1,701M. This is rounding only.
- The FY26 FCF guide path runs $4.5-5.0B (Dec 2025) -> $5.0-5.5B (Jan 28) -> $6.5-7.5B (Apr 22) -> $11.5-12.5B (Jul 22). The $6.5-7.5B figure dates from the Q1'26 release (2026-04-22), which confirms the '(Q1'26?)' in the task.
- MarketScreener's 'Net debt' row is gross cash (FY25 -$8,848M equals cash including restricted cash), so it should not feed the bridge.
