## PP — dated proof points inside the horizon (2026-10-02 to ~2027-10-01) and the current expectation at each

**Headline:** Three company events fall inside the horizon, and the filings and transcripts date them. Q3'26 reports 2026-10-28, five days after the finals. The January 2027 call brings FY26 actuals, the first FY27 outlook, the 2026 margin-in-backlog page and the date of the Capital Markets Day. That CMD, in spring 2027, sets the 2030 outlook. Q3 consensus already sits at the top of the range implied by the guidance, and management says the January margin-in-backlog page mostly speaks to 2029-30 margins, not 2027.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** Of 45 facts, 43 are verified. PP-39 is corrected: Siemens Energy figures are in EUR, and the Gas Services margin is 648/3,756 = 17.25%. PP-44 is unverifiable only on the Section 232 wind outcome; the Commerce report deadline, about 2026-05-10, has passed and no outcome was found. The primary sources I re-opened match: the 10-Q/10-K RPO and unit tables, the Q2'26/Q1'26/Q3'25/Q4'25 releases, the realignment xlsx (fetched live), the Laguna and Q4'25 transcripts (parsed PDFs), the Q2'26 transcript in the corpus, the CFO 8-K, and a Yahoo price series that reproduces every move and drawdown. Minor fixes: the Laguna locators for PP-03/07 are p.10, and the data-center quote in PP-15 is p.6. The PP-33 cross-check should use $3,316M for 2025, not 8.2M x $406. PP-35 should note that the January release also raised FCF guidance. MarketScreener has direct Q4'26 figures ($13,905M / $2,535M, 18.2%) that confirm PP-20, and a Q3 EPS of $4.17. In the method audit: the 'consensus at top of guide' test rests on an analyst-chosen Electrification margin and Corporate split. The RPO-% trend is confounded by the Prolec consolidation. And GW shipped is not the same measure as management's output run-rate commitment. ### Tests

| test | method | result | if long | if short |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Does Q3'26 consensus leave room above the guidance? | Build the Q3'26 range from each segment's guide on the recast Q3'25 base (PP-12..PP-18) and compare it with MarketScreener consensus (PP-19). | Guide-implied revenue is $11,757-12,134M and consensus is $12,013M, in the upper half. Guide-implied adjusted EBITDA is $1,535-1,716M and consensus is $1,706M, within $10M of the top. Consensus already assumes the top of the guidance. Caveat: two assumptions (Electrification margin, Corporate) and possible definition mismatch. | A Q3 'beat' needs more than the top of the guidance; hitting guidance only matches consensus. Q3 prints 10-28, after the finals, so it cannot be the in-competition proof point. | Q3 has little cushion; a mid-range Power margin (17.5%) or Electrification at 18.4% would land below consensus EBITDA. |
| How hard is the Q4'26 hurdle that consensus implies? | FY26 consensus minus H1 actuals minus Q3 consensus (PP-20); Wind Q4 needed to meet the FY guide (PP-21). | The implied Q4'26 margin is 18.1% on $13.8B revenue, against an FY26 guide of 12-14% and H1 at 10.5%. Wind needs about +$257M in Q4 after -$657M in H1 and ~0 in Q3. | The January print tests whether a Q4 margin of ~18% happens. It shows up only in FY26 actuals, reported in January 2027. | The Q4 run-rate carries FY26 consensus. A Wind Q4 shortfall is a specific, dated miss risk (Q4'25 Wind was -$225M). |
| Is the January margin-in-backlog page a proof point for 2027 margins? | Read management's own framing (PP-06, PP-11) against the vintage analysis in the verified state. | No. The CEO says the page 'tells you the equipment margins for '29 and '30, largely'. Separately, management says most higher-margin 2024-25 orders begin delivering in 2027 and later. The page is new information about 2029-30. For 2027 the direct tests are the FY27 guide (January) and the Q1'27/Q2'27 equipment gross margin and Power margin. | The page supports out-year durability, not the FY27 number. It cannot serve as a pass mark for a 2027-margin claim. | A GLJ-style 2027 margin call is tested by the FY27 guide and the 1H'27 prints, not by the backlog-margin page. |
| Does a guidance raise produce a positive stock reaction? | Close-to-close moves on report days since the spin (PP-36). | No reliable link. The Q2'26 print raised FY revenue and FCF guidance, and the stock fell 8.69% on the day and 12.55% over 5 days. Q1'26 (also a raise) rose 13.75%. The Dec 2025 investor update moved +0.55%. | A thesis resting on 'they will raise again' has a weak event record. The magnitude against expectations is what moved the stock. | Raises have not protected the stock. The two -8.6% days (Q2'26 and GLJ) show how large a one-day move can be. |
| Was the January 2026 guidance raise organic? | Read the 2026-01-28 release wording (PP-35). | No. $41-42B -> $44-45B (2026) and $52B -> $56B (2028) came from including Prolec GE (~$3B and ~$4B). The later 2026 raises (+$0.5B in April, +$1.0B in July) were largely organic; Prolec moved only $3.0B -> $3.1B. | Guidance-history evidence should cite the April and July raises. The 2028 $56B framework is unchanged ex-Prolec since first issue. | The 'raised at every report' pattern overstates organic momentum at the January 2026 event. |
| Is the equipment RPO conversion rate holding? | Compare 1- and 2-year conversion percentages across four filings (PP-27). | The 1-year % drifted 41 -> 37 -> 38 -> 36 and the 2-year % 71 -> 69 -> 69 -> 65 while RPO grew 62% ($54.1B -> $87.8B). Dollars converting in the next 12 months still grew: 54,092 x 41% = 22,178 vs 87,821 x 36% = 31,616. The 9/30/26 percentages will print in the Q3 10-Q on 10-28. | Dollar conversion is rising, and a falling % is consistent with longer-dated slots being added. | If the 1-2-year % keeps falling, more of the book lands after 2027. That changes the vintage mix for the 2027 test. |
| Gas shipments against the 5 GW/quarter commitment | Compare 10-Q GW shipped (PP-23) with management's cadence (PP-22). | The last print was 3.3 GW (Q2'26), below Q2'25's 5.2 GW; H1'26 was 7.5 GW against H1'25's 8.2 GW. Management committed to 5 GW in each of Q3 and Q4'26, similar in early 2027, and 6 GW a quarter sometime in H2'27. Q3'25 comparable: 4.0 GW. | Q3 (10-28), Q1'27 and Q2'27 each give a measurable GW line against a stated number. | H1'26 shipments were down YoY. A Q3 print below 5 GW would be the first miss against an explicit capacity commitment. |

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- Test 1 (Q3 room above guide): the arithmetic reproduces. The 'consensus at top of guide' conclusion depends on the analyst-chosen top end: Elec 19.5% is an arbitrary reading of 'modestly above 18.4%', and Corporate -106 assumes an even H2 split, although management says corporate costs are 'uneven across quarters'. Corporate is a residual that also contains Financial Services and eliminations, so it may not map to the $450-500M 'corporate costs' guide. MarketScreener's EBITDA definition is not stated. Say 'consensus ~ at the top of an analyst-constructed range', not 'assumes the top of guidance'. The if_short sensitivity checks out: all-mid gives 1,627, Power 17.5% with the rest high gives 1,678, and Elec 18.4% with the rest high gives 1,672. All are below 1,706.
- Test 2 (Q4 hurdle): the residual method mixes an FY consensus snapshot with a quarterly snapshot. MarketScreener's own Q4'26 row ($13,905M / $2,535M = 18.2%) gives the conclusion directly and should replace the residual. Comparing a Q4 margin with the FY 12-14% guide is apples-to-oranges; note that the FY consensus of 6,359/46,282 = 13.7% is inside the guide. The Wind +$257M arithmetic is correct.
- Test 3 (margin-in-backlog page): supported by Laguna p.5 and p.10 and Q4'25 p.4. Sound.
- Test 4 (raise vs reaction): all ten price moves reproduce. With n=10, raw close-to-close returns, no market or peer adjustment, and no measure of the size of each raise against expectations, it can only support 'no reliable link'. It cannot support a causal read.
- Test 5 (Jan raise organic?): correct for revenue. It omits that the same release raised 2026 FCF by $0.5B and 2028 cumulative FCF by $2B without attributing either to Prolec. 'Largely organic' for the April and July raises is right for revenue, since Prolec moved only $3.0B to $3.1B.
- Test 6 (RPO conversion %): the numbers verify. Confound not mentioned: Prolec backlog entered consolidated RPO from Q1'26 (the acquisition closed Feb 2026), which changes the mix. Each quarter's 1-year window also covers a different calendar period, so the % drift is not a clean like-for-like trend.
- Test 7 (gas shipments vs 5 GW): the numbers verify. Caveat: management's commitment is an output 'run rate' ('hit the five-gigawatt run rate'), while the 10-Q line is GW shipped (sales in units). A Q3 shipped print below 5.0 GW is not automatically a miss of the commitment. The team should set the pass mark knowing the two differ.
- Corrections-to-verified-state: all four are confirmed (OCF $10,680M in the 10-Q; recast FY25 Power 19,873.0 / 2,935.7 / 14.77% and services share 66.36%; recast 12/31/25 segment equipment RPO summing to 64,325.9 vs 64,245; Jan-2026 revenue raise = Prolec; 7.5 GW = 4.2 + 3.3). One addition to the guidance-history point: the Jan-2026 FCF raises.
- Unresolved item 1 is partly resolvable: MarketScreener shows Q3'26 EPS $4.17 (single source, fetched 2026-09-29).

### Unresolved

- Q3'26 EPS consensus could not be verified; aggregator snippets show $4.07 and $4.15 with no primary page retrieved.
- MarketScreener's Q3'26 'EBITDA' ($1,706M) may not use GEV's adjusted-EBITDA definition. It is single-sourced.
- GEV Q4'26, Q1'27 and Q2'27 report dates are not announced; the table uses cadence estimates from prior years. The CMD date is to be set on the January call, and the 2027 AGM date is not announced.
- GEV has never defined 'margin in backlog' (gross, contribution or other). The ~6%/~17% Power levels in PP-10 rest on an analyst's framing that the CEO confirmed only 'directionally'.
- Hyperscaler Q3'26 report dates were not announced as of 2026-09-29. The capex guide figures are from a press aggregator (TMT Finance), not the primary releases.
- PJM 2029/30 BRA exact bidding and results dates come from ESAI (secondary). PJM's primary text says only 'December'.
- The MHI 1H FY2026 results date is not posted, and no successor medium-term-plan date is announced.
- Section 232 wind-turbine outcome: no decision found. Whether any Section 122 or other replacement tariffs followed the IEEPA ruling was not checked.
- The Laguna reference to Power's '$24 billion of revenue' is ambiguous (2026E vs run-rate); it is not used here.
- The Power FY27 consensus margin (20.7%, from GLJ's characterisation) still has no independent source.
- The Corporate cost guide ($450-500M) was assumed to map to the 'Corporate and other' line in the adjusted-EBITDA table; not confirmed.

### Corrections to the verified state

- KNOWN BAD FIGURE, re-sourced. H1'26 operating cash flow of $10,680M IS in the Q2'26 10-Q (0001996810-26-000148): the cash-flow statement line 'Cash from (used for) operating activities 10,680' and the MD&A FCF reconciliation p.32. It is also in the Q2'26 release. The '$14,087M increase in deposits' should be replaced by one of two figures. Note 9 gives 'Contract liabilities and deferred income increased $14,088 million', which includes the Prolec acquisition; the total balance moved 25,950 -> 40,037 = 14,087. The operating cash-flow line gives $13,695M ('$13.7 billion' in the MD&A). For cash deposits, use $13,695M.
- Segment basis mismatch. FY25 Power $19,767M / $2,902M / 14.68% (and the FY23/FY24 margins, the 66.1% services share and the 12/31/24 segment equipment RPO) are on the pre-2026 segment structure. The H1'26 17.63% and Q2'26 18.82% are on the realigned structure. GEV's recast FY25 Power figures are revenue $19,873.0M, EBITDA $2,935.7M, margin 14.77%, services share 66.36% (realignment supplement xlsx, 2026-04-22). The recast 12/31/25 segment equipment RPO is Power $24,707M, Electrification $30,508M, Wind $9,112M, which sums to $64,326M against consolidated $64,245M.
- Guidance history nuance. The 2026-01-28 move from $41-42B to $44-45B (2026) and from $52B to $56B (2028) was the inclusion of Prolec GE (~$3B and ~$4B), not an organic raise. The release says 'which now includes the Prolec GE acquisition'. 'Raised or reaffirmed at every report' is literally true, but the organic raises are April (+$0.5B) and July (+$1.0B).
- Minor: H1'26 gas shipments of 7.5 GW are correct per the 10-Q (4.2 + 3.3). The press releases' rounded '4 GW' and '3 GW' sum to 7, so cite the 10-Q MD&A units table, not the releases.

## POS: Is the insight already in the price, and how is GEV positioned? (price reaction to GLJ, prior use of vintage/RPO analysis, sell-side reactions, ownership/insider/short interest)

**Headline:** GEV's 8.6% drop on the GLJ day (2026-09-14) was mostly a sector move. The same day, the model provider's CEO called for an AI slowdown and the 10-year yield hit 5%. VRT, ETN and Siemens Energy fell 7.6-8.0%. GEV's excess move against a beta-adjusted peer model was -3.3% (t=-1.36), and by 09-29 the cumulative excess move was about +0.6%. The vintage idea is not new: management already discloses "change in margin in backlog" (Power +11 pts in 2025, +17 pts since YE22), Wolfe asked about it on the Q4'25 call, and GLJ built its bear case on those same disclosures. So the team's RPO-timing work is a different reading of shared data, not new information. Positioning: short interest is 3.3% of shares, the hedge-fund holder count fell 118 -> 106 in Q2, there were no insider buys, and small insider sales totalled about $11.5M.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** All 34 facts were checked against the corpus, the SEC filings, the Laguna PDF and the web sources: 29 verified, 1 corrected, 3 definition-mismatch, 1 unverifiable. Prices, returns, the market-model regression (beta 0.706, AR -3.30%, t -1.36, CAR +0.58%), every RPO schedule, the transcript quotes (Q4'25 call and Laguna), the buyback, the short interest and the insider figures all reproduce exactly. Corrected: POS-33 and corrections item 1. The 'KNOWN BAD FIGURE' $14,087M IS in the Q2'26 10-Q: the Note 9 total contract liabilities and deferred income went from 25,950 to 40,037 (narrative $14,088M). It is a balance-sheet change that includes the Prolec acquisition, not a cash-flow deposit line, which is $13,695M. Definition-mismatch: POS-15 (the backlog-margin change is a whole-stock measure, not a per-vintage margin), POS-22 (the SA $7.5-8B at 22x cannot produce a $1,150 target, so it is not comparable to GLJ's $7.42B) and the POS-29 wording (code M is RSU/PSU conversion, not option exercise). Unverifiable: POS-25's 'consensus unchanged over the GLJ window', since there is no pre-GLJ snapshot. Conclusion risks: the SPY-only model makes the 09-14 abnormal return significant (t -2.75), and 247wallst calls GLJ 'the differentiator', so 'mostly a sector move' depends on the benchmark. The CAR is statistically meaningless (SD about 8.4%) and is confounded by the Laguna day. The short-term borrowings gap is resolved: there is no ST debt line, and current maturities are $55M.

### Tests

| test | method | result | if long | if short |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Was the 09-14 drop GEV-specific (GLJ) or sector-wide? | Raw return vs an equal-weight VRT/ETN/Siemens Energy basket, plus a market model (252-day OLS of GEV on a VRT+ETN basket, and on SPY) | The basket fell 7.76% vs GEV's 8.62%. The market-model abnormal return was -3.30% (t=-1.36), not significant. The same day had an AI-slowdown call, a 5% 10-year yield and Siemens AG stake-sale news (ENR). | The GLJ 'overhang' is small in price terms, so a long cannot claim a GLJ-driven discount that will close. The upside has to come from somewhere else. | The market did not validate GLJ on its own merits. The day's move was mostly AI-capex and rates beta, which a short would be exposed to in both directions. |
| How much of the drop has reversed? | Price recovery and cumulative abnormal return 09-14..09-29 | 106% of the raw drop has been regained. The cumulative abnormal return is +0.58% (basket model) and +0.13% (SPY model). The stock sits about where it was on 09-11. | There is no 'post-GLJ dislocation' left to buy. The entry is at the pre-GLJ level. | The market shrugged GLJ off within about 7 sessions. A short needs a new catalyst, not the GLJ thesis. |
| Is the vintage/margin-timing idea already known to the market (the ASML test)? | Search filings, call transcripts, conference transcripts, press and Seeking Alpha for backlog-margin and RPO-timing analysis | Yes. Management discloses 'change in margin in backlog' every January (Power +11 pts FY25, +17 since YE22). Wolfe asked about it on the Q4'25 call. GLJ's bear case uses those disclosures directly. A Seeking Alpha writer modelled 2027 EBITDA at $7.5-8B. The RPO 1/2/5-year schedule is in every 10-Q. No one was found applying the RPO dollar schedule to split 2027 revenue by vintage. | The rebuttal is a reading of shared data. Its value lies in the specific RPO-dollar arithmetic, which conflicts with GLJ's '2025 vintage ships 2028' assumption, not in the concept. Expect judges to know margin-in-backlog. | GLJ's timing claim is exposed to management's own statement that higher-margin '24-'25 orders begin delivering in '27. |
| Does management's timing language agree with the team's vintage cut? | Compare the Q4'25 call (01-28) and Laguna (09-16) statements with the team's RPO-derived 2027 mix | Mixed. On 01-28 management said the majority of higher-margin '24/'25 orders deliver in '27 and beyond, consistent with a large 2025 vintage in 2027. On 09-16 it said the 2026 margin step-change shows mostly in '29-'30 'because that's most of when that equipment delivers'. That sits in tension with the team's 29-35% share for 2026+ vintages in 2027 unless the 2026+ share is short-cycle (Electrification, aeroderivative) revenue. Unresolved. | The team must reconcile the Laguna '29/'30 quote before relying on the 2026+ vintage share. | If 2026+ vintages contribute less to 2027 than the RPO split implies, 2027 leans more on the 2024-25 vintages. |

The error band is about ±10%. | Any vintage-share claim should carry about ±10% uncertainty. | Same: the tool is not precise enough to prove a narrow EBITDA gap. |

There were no Form 4s after 06-03. The only rating actions were reiterations (Bernstein $1,298). The MarketScreener mean is unchanged at $1,237.34. No downgrade or target cut after GLJ was found. | The crowd is still long and bullish. With 30 Buys the upside needs a beat that consensus has not already booked. | Short interest is low (3.3%, 4.6 days), so there is little squeeze risk, but also no sign of a building bear camp. |


### Grids (team's choice)

Team's choice: the pass mark for the January 2027 in-horizon proof point (Power change in margin in backlog for 2026, which management committed to show at the January call). Anchors: FY25 Power +11 pts (Q4'25 call); Laguna guide '26 > '25.

| Power Δ margin-in-backlog 2026 (pts) | Relative to mgmt's Laguna claim | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| < 8 | below FY25's 11 | contradicts management's statement |
| 8-11 | ≤ FY25 | fails the 'larger than '25' test |
| 11-14 | just above FY25 | meets the claim |
| > 14 | well above | beats the claim |

Team's choice: the pass mark for the 'change in margin in backlog' dollars. Anchors: FY25 +$8B (release); management said 'at least as much' in '26.

| Equipment margin-in-backlog added in 2026 | Reading |
|---|---|
| < $8B | below management's floor |
| $8-10B | meets |
| > $10B | beats |

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- Test 1 (sector vs GLJ): the basket-model AR of -3.30% is 38% of GEV's -8.62% raw drop. But the SPY-only model gives AR -7.80% with t = -2.75, which is significant. The lane reports the SPY AR without its t-stat and leans only on the basket result. The conclusion depends on the benchmark choice. The press also disagrees: 247wallst says 'the analyst note is the differentiator rather than the sector move'. 'Mostly a sector move' is defensible only as 'about 60% explained by a VRT/ETN basket'. It should not be stated flat.
- Test 1: ENR is kept in the raw basket after being flagged as contaminated, and its Xetra close is not synchronous with the NYSE close. The ex-ENR basket is -7.61% (GEV minus = -1.01pp); report this as the primary figure. The market model already excludes ENR (fine).
- Test 2 (reversal): CAR +0.58% over 12 days has an SD of about 2.43% x sqrt(12) = 8.4%, so t is about 0.07. It is statistically indistinguishable from zero and from a full persistence of -3.3%. The post-event CAR (09-15..09-29) is +3.88%, and more than 3.5 pts of it comes from the Laguna day (09-16 AR +3.52%), a confounding event. 'Shrugged off' is a price observation, not an inference.
- Test 3 (already known): the evidence holds. One citation is weak: the Seeking Alpha $7.5-8B EBITDA cannot be company-level FY27 EBITDA at 22x giving $1,150 (implies ~$665/sh). Drop it as 'close to GLJ'. Also, management's 'change in margin in backlog' is a whole-stock measure, not a per-vintage margin. Equating GLJ's '3 vs 10-11 pts by vintage' with management's KPI (corrections item 3) is a definitional stretch.
- Test 4 (timing): the Laguna '29/'30 sentence covers company-wide equipment in all three businesses, and p.10 adds 'largely'. It describes where the 2026 margin increment lands. It does not say 2026 bookings contribute nothing to 2027 revenue, so the tension is weaker than stated. The 2026+ share of 2027 is still a residual and not directly observable.
- Test 5 (RPO back-test): this is not a test of the conversion schedule. Actual equipment revenue includes in-year book-and-ship orders that were not in the opening RPO, so actual/predicted above 100% is expected. 2024's 91.8% therefore understates the slippage (cancellations, Wind delays, FX) in the backlog piece. The rounded whole-number percentages add about ±1% ($200M on $40B). The '±10% band' label mixes two effects.
- Test 6 (positioning): 'MarketScreener mean unchanged' cannot be tested because there is no pre-GLJ snapshot (09-18 is post-GLJ). MarketBeat's 2 Sells likely include the suspect MS Underweight row. Short interest of 3.3% is 'of float' per MarketBeat; 3.29% is of shares outstanding. Label which one is used.
- Corrections item 1 is itself wrong: $14,087M reproduces exactly from the Q2'26 10-Q Note 9 table (40,037-25,950), and the narrative says $14,088M. Do not strike it. Relabel it as the H1 change in total contract liabilities and deferred income (a balance-sheet change that includes Prolec acquired balances), distinct from the $13,695M cash-flow line.
- Open item 'short-term borrowings not checked' is resolved: the Q2'26 balance sheet has no short-term borrowings line, and Note 14 shows current maturities of $55M (total borrowings $2,849M). The EV multiples are unchanged at one decimal.

### Unresolved

- GLJ's own report was not seen; all GLJ figures come from press summaries (GuruFocus/Yahoo, Investing.com, Benzinga). Whether its 38.9x is on FY26 or FY27 EBITDA is inferred (38.5x reproduces on FY26).
- The source of GLJ's 'consensus 20.7% Power 2027 margin' is still unknown; no independent consensus segment-margin figure was found.
- The MarketBeat rows 'Morgan Stanley (Percoco) Overweight -> Underweight 8/3/2026' and 'Goldman Sachs Buy -> Market Outperform 9/14/2026' are uncorroborated and likely aggregator errors. MarketScreener shows Morgan Stanley Overweight $1,350 after Q2, and Morgan Stanley's GEV analyst is David Arcaro (Laguna host). Do not cite them.
- The Jefferies $1,185 target date conflicts (09-11 / 09-14 / 09-17).
- 13F aggregate data is unreliable: 13f.info shows 696M shares held in Q2'26, above 266M shares outstanding. Institutional ownership % was not verified. The hedge-fund count 118->106 is single-source (Insider Monkey).
- Siemens Energy closes are single-source (stockanalysis), in EUR and not FX-adjusted, and the stock had its own stake-sale news on 09-14.
- The Q3'26 buyback amount is unknown until the Q3 10-Q (due with results 2026-10-28). Management has signalled Q3 purchases.
- Not searched exhaustively: X/Twitter and Reddit threads (no hits on RPO-percentage analysis via web search), and Seeking Alpha article bodies (paywalled/403).
- Per-year Power margin-in-backlog for 2023 and 2024 is not disclosed; only the combined ~6 pts (17-11) can be derived.
- How to reconcile Strazik's 09-16 statement (2026 orders deliver mostly '29-'30) with the team's RPO-derived 29-35% share of 2027 equipment revenue from 2026+ vintages is still open.
- Short-term borrowings at 2026-06-30 were not checked (Note 14 not read), so the net-cash figure in the EV bridge uses long-term borrowings only.

### Corrections to the verified state

- The 'KNOWN BAD FIGURE' is partly wrong in the other direction. H1'26 operating cash flow of $10,680M IS in the Q2'26 10-Q (0001996810-26-000148, Consolidated Statement of Cash Flows: 'Cash from (used for) operating activities 10,680 1,528'). The contract-liability line there is $13,695M ('Increase (decrease) in contract liabilities and current deferred income 13,695'), not $14,087M; the balance-sheet change is $14,170M (39,944 - 25,774). Strike $14,087M only.
- The corpus prices table is NOT stale at 2025-09-18. It runs to 2026-09-18 for GEV, SPY, VRT, ETN and others, and matches stockanalysis.com to the cent for 2026-09-08..09-18.
- GLJ's backlog-margin framing: the press says the 3 pts vs 10-11 pts are backlog-margin accretion by order vintage, with the 2025 vintage 'shipping in 2028' and 2027 deliveries 'ordered in 2024'. This matches management's own 'change in margin in backlog' KPI (Power +11 pts FY25). It is not a statement about the P&L margin level of shipments.
- The verified state treats the 09-14 drop of -8.6% as a GLJ effect. Peers fell 7.6-8.0% that day on an AI-slowdown call and a 5% 10-year yield. GEV's market-model abnormal return was -3.3% (t=-1.36), and the cumulative abnormal return was about 0 by 09-29.
- The MarketScreener consensus target $1,237.34 (37 analysts, low $940) excludes GLJ; MarketBeat's $1,171.87 includes it. State which aggregator is used.

## TP: gas-turbine / combined-cycle pricing by contract year, from public primary evidence (utility dockets, SEC filings of utilities, OEM and customer remarks)

**Headline:** One GE-specific series compares the same utility, site, unit size and turbine family over time (LG&E/KU, GE 7HA). All-in plant cost went from about $1,398/kW (2022 CPCN) to $2,144-2,194/kW (2025 CPCN), up about 53-57%. By July 2026 the utility cites market indications of $3,000-3,200/kW. The GE power-island deal was signed 2026-06-23 at "updated commercial pricing" that pushed cost above the 2025 estimate. Across utilities the evidence is plant-level, not turbine-only, and uneven: framework customers such as Entergy Mississippi (0%) and Duke (+5%) show flat 2024-25 costs.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** I checked 36 facts: 32 verified, 3 corrected (TP-08, TP-29, TP-36), 1 definition-mismatch (TP-13). Every dollar figure and $/kW calculation recomputes. The problems are in interpretation.

(1) TP-08 leaves out that the $25M GE slot reservation for Brown 12 was paid 'to lock in firm pricing for the equipment' (KY PSC Order p.10). Together with the MC6 Gantt ('GT & Generator' reserved in Aug 2025; only the 'Balance of OEM Scope' refreshed), this undercuts Test 2 and the headline's claim that the GE deal pushed cost above the 2025 estimate. Rpt#3 does not attribute the overrun to GEV, and EPC bids arrived in the same period.

(2) The '2022' vintage label on Mill Creek 5 is wrong. The $902M is a 2023 estimate, and GE's MC5 7HA.03 order was announced 2024-04-11. The pair is therefore a pre-2025-order vs 2026-PI comparison. It also includes 3-4 years of escalation to later in-service dates.

(3) TP-13 compares overnight market quotes for projects without reservations against a nominal CPCN total. The utility says MC6 itself stays below the $3,000-3,200/kW benchmarks.

(4) The Test 5 medians double-count Legend. The 2024->2025 step ranges from +2% to +12% depending on which Legend figure is used, not a firm +7%.

(5) Entergy's exclusivity agreement 'does not establish final pricing', which weakens the claim that frameworks cap price.

(6) The projects table does link Duke Person County to GE Vernova (no price).

(7) The MC6 Power Island Agreement public filing (1,054 pp.) was scanned. Every page is stamped 'CONFIDENTIAL INFORMATION REDACTED', and no price text could be extracted.

Minor fixes: PDF vs printed page locators (Tummonds, GridLab), the NextEra quote wording, the MHI quote truncation, the 'nominal' label on NREL ATB (the report says 'moderate scenario'), the Person County approval date (it comes from Duke FY24 10-K 0001326160-25-000072), and the exhibit accession suffixes for NRG/Vistra. Temporary PDFs were auto-saved by WebFetch under <session-files>

The numerator is an OVERNIGHT indicative cost for projects WITHOUT reservations. The denominator is a CPCN nominal total for a 2031 ISD, which likely includes escalation and possibly AFUDC. The bases differ. The utility also states that MC6 itself stays below these benchmarks, so the % is not MC6's own increase. |

See grid. | Implied turbine/power-island change ranges from +63% to +195% depending on assumptions (team's choice). No primary source discloses a turbine-only price. ### Grids (team's choice)

**Implied turbine/power-island price change for a +55% all-in plant cost change (LG&E/KU pair, TP-05). Team's choice of inputs.**

Formula: g = (1.55 − (1−t)(1+n))/t − 1. Here t = turbine share of the OLD plant cost; n = inflation on non-turbine cost (EPC, BOP, transformers, labour).

| t \ n | n = 20% | n = 35% | n = 50% |
|---|---|---|---|
| t = 20% | +195% | +135% | +75% |
| t = 25% | +160% | +115% | +70% |
| t = 30% | +137% | +102% | +67% |
| t = 40% | +108% | +85% | +63% |

WoodMac (TP-33) gives a 20-30% turbine share of CC project cost. No primary source gives n.

**All-in CC $/kW by filing year (plant level, mixed definitions; see facts)**

| Filing year | n | Median $/kW | Range | Datapoints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2022 | 3 | 1,398 | 1,276-1,533 | OCAPS fixed price, Mill Creek 5, Brown 12 (2022 est.) |
| 2024 | 6 | 2,029 | 1,583-2,255 | Legend (orig and cap), Delta Blues, McNew, Viola, Franklin Farms |
| 2025 | 6 | 2,169 | 1,592-2,344 | Brown 12, MC6, Cayuga, Jefferson, Vicksburg, Anderson |
| 2026 | 2 | 2,731 | 2,688-2,773 | Waterford 6, Westlake (single utility) |
| 2026 market quotes | n/a | n/a | 2,800-3,200 | LG&E/KU Updates #2-#3 (indicative, not contracts) |

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- Test 1 (LG&E/KU pair): the arithmetic holds, but the vintage labels are off. The '2022' MC5 cost is a 2023 PHDR estimate, and GE's MC5 7HA.03 order was announced 2024-04-11. So this is a 2024-order (pre-2025 vintage) vs 2026-PI comparison, which fits the team's vintage framework better than '2022'. The +53-57% also includes 3-4 years of escalation to later ISDs (2027 vs 2030/2031). Utility-stated evidence: Brown 12 +40% is 'for a two-year delay'.
- Test 2 (does a reservation lock price?): the conclusion 'price was refreshed at conversion' is NOT supported. (a) The PSC Order p.10 says the Brown 12 URA was paid 'to lock in firm pricing for the equipment'. (b) The MC6 Gantt reservation covers 'GT & Generator'; the refresh row is 'Balance of OEM Scope'. (c) Rpt#3 attributes the cost trend to 'changing market conditions and updated commercial pricing' without naming GEV, and EPC bids arrived 2026-05-29 in the same period. The evidence is consistent with the GT price locked in 2025 and other scope and EPC repriced in 2026. The headline sentence attributing the overrun to the GE deal should be struck.
- Test 3 (framework customers flat): the inference 'frameworks cap price uplift' conflicts with Entergy's own disclosure that the exclusivity agreement 'does not establish final pricing'. Entergy MS $1.2B figures are rounded to $0.1B, and neither Entergy nor Duke names the OEM for these units. Duke Cayuga uses a winter rating and Anderson includes financing. Both biases push toward showing an increase, so +4.9% is roughly an upper bound. The direction of the conclusion (little 2024-25 escalation) survives. The 'framework customer' label is unverified for Entergy MS.
- Test 4 (Entergy LA +70-75%): recomputed correctly. The scope comparability flag is right. Also, Franklin Farms was filed under a Meta data-center ESA with customer contributions, while Waterford 6 may use third-party financing. Both affect cost definitions.
- Test 5 (medians by filing year): Legend is counted twice in 2024 (original $1,936 and cap $2,121), and the cap was set in Sep 2025. Dropping the duplicate gives a 2024 median of $1,936 (original only), so 2025/2024 = +12.0%; or $2,121 (revised only), so +2.3%. The '+7%' conclusion is fragile. The sample also mixes OEMs (Evergy is Mitsubishi; OCAPS/Entergy vendors unknown), winter/summer/unstated MW bases, and inclusion of AFUDC, transmission and financing. It measures plant cost, not GE price.
- Test 6 (decomposition grid): all 12 cells were recomputed and are correct (e.g. t=0.2, n=0.2: (1.55-0.96)/0.2-1=1.95). Method issues: t uses WoodMac's GAS-TURBINE-only 20-30% share but the result is labelled 'turbine/power-island'. GE's MC5 scope included GT, ST, generator and HRSG, so the power-island share is larger than t, which would lower g. The +55% input also embeds escalation to later ISDs, which belongs in n.
- The MC6 Power Island Agreement public PDF (1,054 pp., ~49 MB) was streamed and scanned in memory. It is the 'Equipment Purchase Agreement for Mill Creek Generating Station Unit 6 PIE Equipment Project between GE Vernova Operations LLC ... and Louisville Gas and Electric Company' (DocuSign). Every page is stamped 'CONFIDENTIAL INFORMATION REDACTED'. Article 8 (8.1 Contract Price p.46, 8.3 Progress Payments p.49) appears in the TOC, but no price or escalation text was extractable. The document does not resolve pricing.

### Unresolved

- The LG&E/KU-GE Vernova Power Island Agreement (public/redacted version) was filed 2026-09-03 in KY PSC Case 2025-00313: https://psc.ky.gov/pscecf/2025-00313/rick.lovekamp%40lge-ku.com/09032026035125/04-PSC_DR1_LGE_KU_Attach_to_Q1_-_Public_Power_Island_Agreement_MC6-compressed.pdf. It exceeded the fetch size limit (>10 MB) and was NOT read. It may show escalation, payment milestones or scope. The team should open it manually.
- No primary source found gives a turbine-only or power-island-only $/kW for any contract year. Every docket figure is all-in plant cost, which includes EPC, transformers, interconnection and sometimes AFUDC/financing.
- The KY PSC order chart ($2,120/$2,138 per kW) does not reconcile with $1,383M/$1,415M divided by 645 MW ($2,144/$2,194). The basis is not stated (possibly overnight or ex-AFUDC). Not resolved.
- Entergy's exclusivity vendor is not named in any filing. The OEMs for Duke Cayuga and Anderson are not named in the 10-Q. The Duke-GEV framework (Apr 2025) suggests 7HA, but project-level OEM is unverified.
- Georgia Power Bowen/Wansley/McIntosh $/kW are redacted in public filings. Only the qualitative 'more than doubled since the 2025 IRP' is public.
- Duke Person County CC cost was confidential in the CPCN. A ~$1.96B figure seen in a search snippet is an external estimate and was not verified.
- Evergy Viola total cost ($1,577.5M) is inferred by doubling the 50% share. McNew's doubling is corroborated by GridLab's $1,601.0M.
- Franklin Farms (Oct 2024) and Waterford 6/Westlake (Feb 2026) may have different scope. FF excludes the $546M line; whether WF6/Westlake include transmission was not verified.
- A BNP Paribas estimate of ~$950/kW for HA-class CC turbines (mid-2026), seen only in a search summary, was not verified.
- The Siemens Energy quotes come from a third-party transcript (Investing.com), not a company-hosted transcript. The NextEra $785 vs $2,400/kW figures come from press coverage of CERAWeek, not a filing.
- GEV revenue-recognition timing for reservation-then-PI orders (for example, whether MC6 enters RPO at the 2026-06-23 PI date) is not disclosed in any source reviewed.

---

## RQ — Risk register and judge-question bank

**Headline:** Built from filings, the risk register finds three risks that show up in the reported numbers inside the horizon. First, H2'26 guidance needs a step-up: implied adj EBITDA margin 13.2-16.7% vs 10.5% in H1, and Wind has to earn +$257M in H2 after losing $657M in H1. Second, operating cash flow depends on deposits: excluding the contract-liability inflow it was -$3.0B in both FY25 and H1'26. Third, reservation conversion: 54% of the 116 GW is SRAs, against 27% at Siemens Energy. The 'known bad' OCF figure is resolved: $10,680M is real, and the $14,087M figure is a balance-sheet change that includes Prolec, not an OCF line.

**Confidence:** 4/5 · **Verifier:** I re-derived all 45 facts from the corpus filings, the Laguna transcript PDF, the SE analyst PDF, FactSet, FRED, the Fed release, Yahoo closes and the cited press. Result: 34 verified, 8 corrected, 1 unverifiable, 1 definition-mismatch.

Corrections:
- RQ-07: the separation-cost add-back includes a one-time stock option grant, so some SBC is excluded from adj EBITDA.
- RQ-08: the H2 margin range is 13.2-16.9%, not 13.2-16.7%.
- RQ-20: 7 GW/qtr comes after a period at 6 GW in 2028, not as a 2028 run-rate.
- RQ-26: the Korea Times locator fails; the 12-unit count is confirmed by Doosan's 2026-03-06 release.
- RQ-31: the July and September falls are not among the largest; 2025-12-17 (-10.5%) ranks second.
- RQ-38: the EV should include $55M of current maturities, giving $247,230M.

Other findings:
- RQ-27: Microsoft's capex figure is fiscal-year and lease-basis, so the 2026 sum mixes bases.
- RQ-32: the EBITDA consensus is not in the cited article, and GEV reports no adjusted EPS.
- RQ-35: $3,037M of buyback headroom is stale, because the CEO said Q3 buybacks happened.
- RQ-45: the $530.16 52-week low is intraday; the lowest close is $547.96.

The known-bad OCF resolution is confirmed. The SRA roll-forward 1 GW gap recurs in other quarters, so it is most likely rounding.

### Verifier's audit of the tests

- Known-bad-figure test: conclusion correct. $10,680M OCF is sourced (p.7), the CF deposit line is $13,695M, and $14,087M is the Note 9 table change incl. non-current deferred income and Prolec balances. The balance-sheet current-line change of $14,170M (39,944-25,774) also checks.
- Deposit-dependence test: the arithmetic is right, but 'OCF ex-deposits' is one-sided. It strips the deposit inflow while keeping the working-capital uses the deposits fund (H1'26 inventories -1,744, receivables -843) and one-off items (cash taxes -1,258). The conclusion 'deposits reverse as delivery happens' is imprecise: contract liabilities unwind into revenue, not a cash outflow. The cash hit comes only if new deposits fall short of liquidation. Also, 'FCF after SBC' ignores the H1'26 equity-statement line 'Issuance of shares in connection with equity awards (503)' (FY25 (177)).
- SBC/adj-EBITDA (RQ-07): the claim 'SBC stays a cost inside adj EBITDA' needs a qualifier. The Separation-costs add-back explicitly includes a 'one-time stock option grant', so part of SBC is excluded (amount undisclosed).
- H2 guidance hurdle: the pairings understate the upper bound. The full guidance box spans 13.2%-16.9% (not 16.7%). Wind +$257M, H2 FCF $1.6-2.6B and Grid B (543/46,000 = 1.18 pts) all check.
- Reservation-conversion test: the 1 GW Q2'26 SRA roll gap is presented as a possible lapse, but Q3'25 (SRA 30 vs 29) and Q4'25 (firm 41 vs 40, SRA 42 vs 43) have the same size gaps in both directions. Treat the gap as whole-GW rounding unless a filing says otherwise. The SE comparison is valid on disclosed numbers, but SRA/backlog definitions across companies are not shown to be identical.
- Mix-vs-price test: the sign-test logic depends on an unverified premise that Power services margin exceeds equipment margin (not disclosed). The 10-Q cites favorable pricing for BOTH equipment and services revenue, so 'within-category' improvement could be services pricing, not equipment. The H1 attribution also includes productivity.
- GLJ-reversal test: verified (+0.55% vs 09-11 close). Valid as a price observation only.
- Rates backdrop: co-movement only, as stated. It mixes the 09-28 yield with the 09-29 price (immaterial).
- Competitor capacity test: '7 GW/qtr in 2028' misstates the CEO. 2028 may sit at 6 GW/qtr for a period before stepping to 7, consistent with the 24 GW 2028 guide. The SE '+15 units 2027' refers to production units, not turbines or GW. The Doosan source must be switched to Doosan's 2026-03-06 release.
- Buyback headroom test: $3,037M is as of 06-30. The CEO said Q3 gave 'another good opportunity' to repurchase, so the headroom is overstated today. The 'new or enlarged authorisation likely' conclusion is speculation with no source.
- Largest-one-day-falls framing (RQ-31) is wrong. The 07-22 and 09-14 drops rank ~7th/8th; 2025-12-17 (-10.50%) is the second largest, and its cause is unresolved (already listed).
- Question-bank item 22 relies on RQ-32, whose EBITDA-consensus figure is unsourced and whose EPS consensus basis is unknown (GEV publishes no adjusted EPS). Only the revenue beat and the GAAP EPS figure are defensible.

### Unresolved

- Cause of the -10.5% GEV close on 2025-12-17 not found
- No proof that the Vineyard Wind settlement cost is inside the ~$400M Wind guidance; terms are confidential until the Q3 10-Q (and possibly after)
- The consensus Q2'26 EPS of $3.04 (Investing.com) may be on an adjusted basis different from GEV's GAAP diluted $2.47; basis unverified
- Equipment vs services EBITDA margin within Power is not disclosed, so the mix-vs-price test is a sign test only
- The 1 GW gap in the Q2'26 SRA roll-forward (64 computed vs 63 reported): rounding vs lapse/cancellation not disclosed; no SRA cancellation figure found in any filing
- Customer concentration (>10% customers) not disclosed; data-center counterparty credit not quantifiable from filings
- Doosan and hyperscaler capex figures rest on secondary aggregators (Korea Times/press summary; TMT Finance) and need checking against primary releases
- Liquidated-damages exposure on HA turbines is not quantified in filings, and no HA fleet-quality issue was found in the FY25 10-K or Q2'26 10-Q
- Forward P/E at past troughs could not be computed without historical consensus EPS; Grid A anchors use today's FY27 EPS
- HFAC rule text on AI was not read directly this run (taken from PLAYBOOK.md section 7)

### Corrections to the verified state

- KNOWN BAD FIGURE resolved. 'H1'26 OCF $10,680M' IS in the Q2'26 10-Q (0001996810-26-000148, cash flow statement p.7, and the release FCF table). The $14,087M is NOT an OCF component: it is the change in total contract liabilities and deferred income in the Note 9 table ($40,037M - $25,950M; Note 9 text says $14,088M) and includes Prolec acquired balances. The OCF line is 'Increase (decrease) in contract liabilities and current deferred income 13,695'.
- Diluted weighted-average shares: 270M is the Q2'26 three-month figure; the six-month H1'26 figure is 271M (10-Q income statement). Match the period when dividing.
- The 52-week high of $1,195.94 is intraday. The highest close since the spin is $1,174.86 on 2026-06-30 (Yahoo). Use closes consistently in drawdown math.
- H1'26 buyback: $3,645M excludes commissions and excise taxes (Note 17). The equity statement shows $3,672M and cash paid is $3,671M (cash flow statement). State which one is used.
- Not an error in the verified state, but a trap: press (Utility Dive 2026-08-13) calls GEV's 116 GW 'backlog'. It is 53 GW firm backlog plus 63 GW SRAs, which are not in RPO. Siemens Energy's comparable is 69 GW firm plus 26 GW SRAs.
